## Chapter 17 — Susceptibility and the Honest Box

### The honest box: two frameworks, two organisms [D]

His commission asked for susceptibility "based upon MRSA and non-MRSA." The record answers with a box the manuscript will not cross:

- **MSSA vs MRSA** is a *Staphylococcus aureus* framework. MSSA = methicillin-susceptible *S. aureus*. MRSA = methicillin-resistant *S. aureus*, carrying the *mecA* gene on the mobile element SCC*mec*, producing PBP2a — an additional penicillin-binding protein with decreased affinity for beta-lactams [D].
- **PSSP / PISP / PRSP (or PNSP)** is the *Streptococcus pneumoniae* framework. PSSP = penicillin-susceptible *S. pneumoniae*; PISP = intermediate; PRSP = resistant; PNSP = penicillin-nonsusceptible (intermediate plus resistant) [D].

Laid side by side **without conflating them**: staph has no serotypes (spa typing, MLST, SCC*mec*), so "MRSA serotypes" is not a documented category [D]. His expectation — *"that will tell me all things about what medicines work with each serotype and serotype sickness"* — is filed verbatim as his [H]; the honest line beside it: susceptibility patterns describe the organism in the lab, not the medicine for a person [D]. No treatment recommendations live in this chapter.

**Simply put:** There are two scoreboards and they belong to two different teams. One scores *staph* — the MSSA/MRSA scoreboard, decided by a gene called mecA. The other scores *pneumococcus* — the PSSP/PISP/PRSP scoreboard, decided by how the wall-building enzymes answer penicillin. You can't read the staph scoreboard onto a pneumococcal coat number. The chapter holds them side by side and never merges them.

### The two resistance machines [D]

The record holds two documented machines — the kinship between them is the substrate of his resistance thesis:

- **Machine 1: pneumococcal penicillin resistance.** Penicillin kills by binding penicillin-binding proteins (PBPs 1a, 2b, 2x) — the enzymes that build the cell wall [D]. Resistant pneumococci carry PBPs with reduced affinity — the drug cannot grip its target [D]. The altered *pbp* genes are **mosaics**: native DNA recombined with blocks picked up from related streptococci (*S. mitis*, *S. oralis*), which *S. pneumoniae* splices into its own chromosome because it is naturally transformable [D]. A functional *murM* gene (alternative cell-wall substrate) is required to express the resistance [D]. Selection does the rest: the altered survive and spread as named international clones (Spain23F-1, Spain6B-2) [D]. His framing — "borrowed armor" — is filed as his [H].
- **Machine 2: staphylococcal methicillin resistance.** The *mecA* gene encodes PBP2a, an added substitute protein that keeps building the wall with the drug present — same target class as Machine 1, different route (an added substitute, not a rewritten one) [D].

The documented kinship: both machines work by changing the drug's grip on the wall-building enzymes [D]. The step from there to his thesis is his own.


### The breakpoints the table uses [D]

The CLSI penicillin breakpoints for *S. pneumoniae* (revised 2008) depend on where the drug is working, because the same bacterium in a different compartment faces a different drug concentration [D]:

- **Meningitis (IV penicillin):** Susceptible ≤0.06 µg/mL; no intermediate category; Resistant ≥0.12 µg/mL.
- **Non-meningitis (IV):** S ≤2; I = 4; R ≥8 µg/mL.
- **Non-meningitis (oral):** S ≤0.06; I 0.12–1; R ≥2 µg/mL.

Ceftriaxone/cefotaxime: meningeal S ≤0.5, I = 1, R ≥2; non-meningeal S ≤1, I = 2, R ≥4 µg/mL [D]. Meropenem: S ≤0.25, I = 0.5, R >1 [D]. Multidrug-resistant (MDR) = non-susceptible to three or more classes [D]. The anchor for the whole table: CDC Active Bacterial Core Surveillance, 2024, 3,013 isolates — penicillin 95.8% susceptible, erythromycin 23.9% resistant, levofloxacin 99.8% susceptible, vancomycin 100% susceptible [D].

**The documented pattern [D]:** resistance concentrates where Chapter 15 already said it does. **19A** — the armored one, 93.3% oral-penicillin nonsusceptible, MDR 86.7%. **6B** — only 8.7% fully penicillin-susceptible. **23F** — only 3.8% fully susceptible, the classic resistant colonizer. **14, 19F, 9V** — the rest of the classic set. **11A, 15B, 33F** — the newer armed arrivals. The four predominant MDR serotypes — **14, 19A, 19F, 23F** — account for 58.97% of all MDR isolates in the Dovepress set [D]. The rest of his 23 are documented as generally susceptible, with per-drug numbers thin or absent — honestly marked, not filled.

**The honest limits [GAP]:** (1) per-serotype susceptibility is reported in *studies of specific populations* — not as a universal table; numbers move between populations. (2) **Levofloxacin per-serotype data: thin across the board** — the aggregate (99.8% susceptible, CDC ABCs 2024) is all the record holds. (3) **Ceftriaxone per-serotype data: thin except 19A and the B-study rows** — the aggregate (96.6% S) is the backstop. (4) Serotypes 1, 2, 4, 5, 7F, 8, 9N, 20, 17F carry thin or no per-drug susceptibility data — honestly marked, not filled.


### His resistance theses, verbatim, with the seams marked

**Thesis 1 [H]:** *"There's a relationship between methicillin resistance and each serotype and penicillin and the relationship that those are fungal based upon their methicillin resistance in those serotypes."* (Filed reading: "methadone" = **methicillin**, his correction open.) The factual substrate: penicillin non-susceptibility in pneumococcus does cluster by serotype — ten of his 23 carry documented resistance associations — and the mechanism (altered PBPs) is the same target class that MRSA alters [D]. The step from "resistant like MRSA" to "fungal based upon" is his elevation — his to evidence [H].

**Thesis 2 [H]:** *"All MRSA serotypes are fungal. And I always have a bacteria element."* (Filed readings: "methadone" = methicillin; "I always have" possibly = **"they always have"** — the resistant forms always carry a bacterial element alongside the fungal; his corrections open.) The documented answer: MRSA is *S. aureus*, a bacterium, and staph has no serotypes [D]. His thesis stands marked [H], his to evidence. The company his second thesis keeps: polymicrobial reality is documented — co-infections, biofilm co-aggregation — so "frequently both" is documented while "always both" remains his elevation [H→D, H].

No application is made here of these theses to any person's results. The table is the instrument; the reading of it is his.

### Documentation and Acceptability (appended at his direction)

His commission, his words: *"Please add document and acceptability to the twenty-three serial panel workup you just did."* — "serial" read as **serotype**; "document and acceptability" read as **documentation of sources plus the acceptability/response criteria**. His correction open.

**Part 1 — the source ledger.** Every source class this part stands on, and what it supported [D]:

1. **His panel PDF** — `workspace/user/files/mayoMyChart_-_Test_Results_18_l5ad.pdf` [D]. The source of all 23 quoted values, the per-serotype normal value (>=1.0 mcg/mL), and the lab's own interpretation text (quoted below). Mayo Clinic, *Streptococcus pneumoniae* IgG Antibodies, 23 Serotypes; collected Oct 29, 2025, 4:27 PM; ordered by Rick Klein, D.O.; specimen blood, venous; final result Oct 30, 2025.
2. **The four vaccine compositions** [D]: PPSV23 (1983) — AAP listing; PCV15 (2021) — AAP listing; PCV20 (2021/2023) — Pfizer manufacturer listing, cross-checked to AAP; PCV21 (2024) — DailyMed package listing. Verified against listings, not memory (Oct 17, 2026). The computed coverage counts (13/19/12 of his 23) derive arithmetically from these compositions.
3. **Per-serotype disease associations**, as cited in the rows [D]: CDC MMWR; PMC2587476; the northern Ghana serotype-1 outbreak; the systematic pneumococcal-outbreak review; PMC9764711; Belgian and MDPI serotype-3 failure studies; ScienceDirect 2026; Frontiers 2025; PMC10383425; PMC8492118; PMC12642354; PMC3546102; the Oligbu review via MDPI; Clinical Microbiology and Infection; the Spain WGS clone study; clinicalpub.
4. **The two resistance machines** [D]: mosaic PBP mechanism — PMC 2005, PNAS 2023, and the existing manuscript file *Penicillin-Resistant Serotypes* ("borrowed armor" framing filed as his there too); mecA/PBP2a — ScienceDirect and the mecA literature; staph typing methods (spa/MLST/SCCmec — no serotypes) [D].
5. **The susceptibility table** [D]: A = PMC3372340 (by-serotype PSSP/PISP/PRSP); B = Frontiers in Pediatrics 2024 (U.S. children's middle-ear isolates); C = CDC ABCs 2024; D = Dovepress (MDR serotype distribution); E = Quebec 1996–1998 IPD surveillance; CLSI breakpoints (2008 revision).
6. **The biofilm and reverse-engineering nodes** [D]: Allegrucci et al. 2006, J Bacteriol (architecture groups); Sempere et al. 2024 (the biofilm-five); mBio 2017 (dispersion modulation); PMC2566003 (19F mutation engine); Weinberger, PMC3425340 (CST vs IST); Sharma-Chawla 2016 and Song et al. (influenza head-to-head, ICI pattern).
7. **Existing manuscript companions** [D]: *Serotype × Co-Pathogen Cross-Link Map (2026-10-17)*; *Pneumococcal Parts × Viruses — The Related-Materials Cross-Reference*; *THE GRAND EXPANSION*; *THE FULL TREE* (kept separate from this serotype work).
8. **An honest sourcing limitation:** many row claims carry bare [D] tags — documented in established pneumococcal literature (vaccine trials, CDC Active Bacterial Core surveillance, clone studies) but not individually re-cited in each row. The full audit trail for those rows would require re-verification paper by paper. Marked here as a standing [GAP], not a hidden one.

**Part 2 — the laboratory's own criteria, quoted, not the manuscript's.** Mayo's interpretation text from his PDF [D]:

*"Evaluation of the immune response following pneumococcal vaccination can be assessed by measuring serotype-specific Streptococcus pneumonia IgG antibodies. Either of the following conditions is consistent with a normal response to Streptococcus pneumonia vaccination: 1. When comparing pre and post-vaccination samples, antibody concentrations increased by at least 2-fold for either >50% of serotypes in children <6 years of age or >70% of serotypes for individuals >6 years of age. 2. In either a pre- or post-vaccination sample, antibody concentrations >=1.0 mcg/mL for either >50% of serotypes for children <6 years of age or >70% of serotypes for individuals >6 years of age. Results >=1.0 mcg/mL or those showing a >=2-fold change are consistent with an immune response, but are not necessarily sufficient to provide protection against infection."*

Plain arithmetic on his quoted values against the lab's stated ≥1.0 mcg/mL numeric mark — a count of numbers, **not a verdict**:

1: 0.1 no · 2: 0.3 no · 3: <0.1 no · 4: 0.1 no · 5: <0.1 no · 8: 0.7 no · 9N: 0.1 no · 12F: 0.1 no · 14: 0.1 no · 17F: 0.2 no · 19F: 0.3 no · 20: 0.3 no · 22F: 0.3 no · 23F: 0.1 no · 6B: 0.1 no · 10A: 1.6 **yes** · 11A: 0.1 no · 7F: 0.2 no · 15B: 0.4 no · 18C: 0.5 no · 19A: 0.3 no · 9V: 0.4 no · 33F: 0.5 no

**The count, stated plainly: 1 of 23 serotypes sits at or above the lab's ≥1.0 numeric mark (serotype 10A, 1.6 mcg/mL); 22 of 23 sit below it.** [D — arithmetic on quoted numbers]

What this arithmetic does and does not say: the lab's first condition requires comparing pre- and post-vaccination samples (≥2-fold rise for >70% of serotypes in those over 6) — this file holds one sample and no vaccination record. The lab's second condition counts how many serotypes meet the mark — but "consistent with an immune response" is the lab's phrase, and the lab itself adds "not necessarily sufficient to provide protection." Whether his response is "acceptable" or "normal" is a **clinical judgment**, and this chapter makes none: that judgment belongs to **Kelsey Meyer, NP, at the Oct 19 visit**. No interpretation, no recommendation, no medical advice.

**Simply put:** The lab's own rulebook is printed above, word for word. It asks for two blood draws — before and after a vaccine — and his file has one draw and no vaccine record. So the rulebook can't be fully used here. All the manuscript does is the bare counting: of 23 coats, one number sits at or above the lab's printed mark, twenty-two below. Whether that's "acceptable" is not decided here. That's Monday.

## Chapter 18 — Quest and the Azole Statement

### The commission

**His commission, his words [H]:**

*"Please research susceptibility testing with the Quest Laboratories version of the Mayo Clinic 23 valiant or stereotype test because Quest Laboratories in 2016-27 approximately stated that azole class medicines were treatment for each of those encephalitis positives in the encephalitis testing and I'm cross-referencing that to the streptococcus pneumoniae because if a person gets all those tests at the same time like I did, I have all those positives at the same time for the same human being that's being sick. And all those sicknesses combined with those immunoglobulins and the inclusions and the reactions in between."*

(Filed readings: "valiant"/"stereotype" = valent/serotype; "2016-27" = **2016–2017 approximately**; his corrections open.)

**Simply put:** Douglas asked for two things. First: find Quest's version of the 23-coat test and Quest's susceptibility testing. Second: find a statement he remembers from around 2016–2017 — that Quest said azole medicines were the treatment for each encephalitis positive on encephalitis testing. The chapter found the first and reported the second honestly: the search came back empty, and the empty result is printed as plainly as the found ones.

### Quest's version of the 23-serotype test — found [D]

Quest Diagnostics offers the direct equivalent of Mayo's PN23M:

- **Test code 16963: Streptococcus pneumoniae IgG Antibody (23 Serotypes), MAID** — the Quest version of the 23-serotype panel.
- **Serotypes included:** 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9 (9N), 12 (12F), 14, 17 (17F), 19 (19F), 20, 22 (22F), 23 (23F), 26 (6B), 34 (10A), 43 (11A), 51 (7F), 54 (15B), 56 (18C), 57 (19A), 68 (9V), 70 (33F) — **the same 23 serotypes as Mayo's panel**, matching the PPSV23 composition. (The parenthetical numbers are the Danish nomenclature codes; Quest lists them the same way Mayo does.)
- **Method:** MAID — multi-analyte immunodetection using the Luminex flow cytometric system, measuring all analytes simultaneously. Calibrated against the FDA standard reference serum 89-S. Results reported in µg/mL (mcg/mL).
- Quest also offers smaller panels: **16374** (13 serotypes) and **19564** (14 serotypes), same MAID method.
- Mayo's PN23M, for comparison: bead-based multiplex immunoassay, serum specimen, results in mcg/mL, reference ≥1.0 mcg/mL per serotype.

**A documented difference between the labs, stated plainly [D]:** Quest's own FAQ on these tests (FAQS.181, Version 1, **effective 10/19/2017 — squarely in his timeframe**) notes that while serologic correlates of protection are not rigorously established, WHO-aligned data suggest protection from invasive disease around ≥0.3–0.5 µg/mL for healthy children after conjugate vaccination, and that **expert opinion suggests a cutoff of ≥1.3 µg/mL may be more relevant after pneumococcal polysaccharide vaccines and for immunocompromised patients** — a higher bar than Mayo's ≥1.0. Same test, different lab, different interpretive numbers on the page. The documented record, not a verdict on anyone.

### Quest's susceptibility architecture — and the seam [D]

- **Test code 6641: Susceptibility Panel, Aerobic Bacterium** — on a pure culture isolate on solid agar; method varies (MIC via CPT 87186, disk via 87184, or E-test via 87181) [D]. Quest's stated clinical significance: susceptibility "can indicate the relative success or failure that may be expected if this agent is used to treat that organism" and can guide therapy choice.
- **Test code 651: Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC)** — "Aerobic MIC," microbroth dilution, on pure bacterial culture (room temperature, 48-hour stability; frozen rejected) [D].
- **The honest limit:** no pneumococcus-specific susceptibility panel was found in Quest's directory. Susceptibility is organism-based — it runs on the cultured isolate, not on the serotype or the antibody test [D].

**The seam, stated once [D]:** the serotype antibody test (16963) measures the patient's IgG response from serum; the susceptibility test measures the cultured bacterium's drug response from an isolate. Two different specimens, two different questions. The antibody panel never reports susceptibility, and the susceptibility panel never reports serotypes.


### The 2016–2017 azole statement — not found, reported plainly

**Plainly stated: no source was found [D].** Three separate searches — Quest Diagnostics bulletins, test directory notes, newsroom releases, and education pages from 2016–2017 — turned up no Quest publication, lab bulletin, FAQ, or test interpretation stating that azole-class medicines were treatment for encephalitis positives on encephalitis testing.

What Quest's encephalitis testing actually is, on the documented record [D]:

- **Infectious side — test code 12600: Meningoencephalitis (Encephalitis) Panel, CSF.** Includes California Encephalitis Virus Antibody Panel (IFA), Eastern Equine Encephalitis Virus Antibody, Herpes Simplex Virus 1/2 (IgG), Lymphocytic Choriomeningitis Virus Antibody, Mumps Antibody Panel, St. Louis Encephalitis Virus Antibody, West Nile Virus Antibodies (IgG, IgM), Western Equine Encephalitis Virus Antibody Panel. Note: **"California Encephalitis Virus" sits on Quest's panel by name** — his "California in the cross encephalitis" has a documented address on the lab's own menu.
- **Autoimmune side** — Encephalitis Antibody Evaluation with Reflex to Titer and Line Blot (serum and CSF): 25 antibodies, cell-based assay, NMDA included. No treatment statements attached.

**His account of the statement stands as his [H]** — unverified. If he has the document (a test report, a bulletin, a screenshot), supplying it would close this gap.

**Simply put:** The remembered statement — that Quest once printed that azoles treat each encephalitis positive — could not be found in any Quest publication, bulletin, or page the searches reached. That's the honest result: not-found, not disproven. Douglas's memory of it stands as his, exactly as told. A document would close it.

### The verbal account: the Quest lab head — his words [H]

His account of what was said in person, filed verbatim as his [H] (not independently verified; "Dr. Obeid" = Dr. Obaid, his rendering):

*"When Dr. Obeid and I were doing clinical research, the head of Quest Laboratories Laboratory Department that deals with teaching the labs and the people that flew in told Dr. Obeid that to treat the fungal element underlying all the encephalitises which they all have a fungal element requires azoles and the original testing that we were working with actually allowed for susceptibility to be done to try different azole class drugs against each one. as at that time Quest Laboratories did not recommend any specific medicine for each one as that treatment path is left wide open for the doctor. And the doctor is looking for some type of guidance in these situations once in a while."*

Separated, as his account [H]: (1) the Quest head told Dr. Obaid that **all the encephalitises carry an underlying fungal element** and treating it **requires azoles**; (2) the testing they worked with **allowed susceptibility testing** — trying different azole-class drugs against each one; (3) Quest **did not recommend any specific medicine** — the treatment path **left wide open for the doctor**; (4) his observation: the doctor is looking for guidance in these situations once in a while. This is the verbal-account counterpart to the published-statement hunt that came back not-found. Filed as account and history — **no treatment recommendation is made here**: his account of what was said, not a prescription.

### What the literature says about azoles — kept distinct [D]

Two separate bodies of fact, presented as documented information only — never as recommendations:

**A. Azoles in fungal CNS infections [D].** Azoles inhibit ergosterol synthesis in fungal cell membranes — antifungals, not antivirals. Their documented CNS roles: fluconazole in cryptococcal meningitis (IDSA guidelines — the backbone of consolidation and maintenance); voriconazole has the best CNS penetration among azoles — the documented azole for CNS aspergillosis (CDC's 2012 interim guidance for the contaminated-steroid fungal CNS outbreak recommended it, with liposomal amphotericin B added for severe disease); itraconazole penetrates CSF poorly — documented inferior alternative in cryptococcal maintenance.

**B. Azoles in viral (encephalitis-panel) encephalitis — no established role [D].** Herpes simplex encephalitis: IV acyclovir, 14–21 days — the documented treatment, not an azole. Arboviral encephalitis (West Nile, EEE, SLE, WEE, La Crosse/California serogroup — the organisms on the encephalitis panels): **supportive care; no established antiviral or azole treatment.** Autoimmune encephalitis: immunotherapy (steroids, IVIG, plasma exchange, rituximab) — not azoles.

The honest line: azoles are documented for *fungal* CNS infections; the encephalitis panels test for *viral* (and autoimmune) causes. A statement that azoles treat "each of those encephalitis positives" would only make documented sense if the positives were fungal — and no such Quest statement was found [D]. His account stands as his.


### His trial accounts, condensed — his history [H]

His trial account (October 15 dictation, extended October 17) is his history: his ex-wife verbally agreed to be "the experiment"; Dr. Obaid hired with Medicare/Medicaid money; Hospital Sisters in Illinois gave permission; Quest Laboratories flew in a plane full of people to help. Filed as his account, not independently verified [H]. Two threads from that account, his words:

**The ancient-medicine lists:** *"Quest Laboratories was going to let us use antibiotics that were older, and Dr. Obeid and I were actually working on lists of ancient medicines in an attempt to work forward and backward both. Because we didn't want to, in our clinical trial, favor one medicine or manufacturer, as we wanted to try a bunch of different things. My ex-wife was really sickly, and it would have worked quite well."* Quest was going to let them use older antibiotics; he and Obaid built the lists forward and backward both — his bidirectional method — so the trial would favor no medicine or manufacturer; his belief: it would have worked quite well [H]. The lists themselves are not in the record [GAP — his to dictate].

**The IV+oral plan:** *"We were describing using IV and oral antibiotics and azole-style medicines as my ex-wife. We were trying to secure her type 1 diabetes with Quest Laboratory testing. And we were going to actually attempt to use oral doxycycline and IV doxycycline as with certain medications, like even for myself, I might need some type of IV antifungal to get the pills to work the right way. And I've been trying to get that figured out somehow."* The trial plan: IV and oral antibiotics plus azole-style medicines for her; securing her type 1 diabetes with Quest testing; oral and IV doxycycline [H]. His personal statement at the end — "I might need some type of IV antifungal to get the pills to work the right way" — is filed verbatim without interpretation; it travels to the October 19 visit as his question for Kelsey, her call. No interpretation, no recommendation — the manuscript holds his words and his boundary together.

**Simply put:** Behind the lab tests sits a story from years ago: his ex-wife agreed to be "the experiment," Dr. Obaid ran it, and Quest flew people in. They drew up lists of older antibiotics — forward and backward, so no drug company got favored — and planned IV and oral antibiotics plus azole-style medicines for her, with Quest testing to steady her type 1 diabetes. The lists themselves never made it into the file; they're his to supply if he wants them there. The last sentence — his own question about an IV antifungal for himself — is printed as his words and carried to Monday as his question. His question, her answer.

## Chapter 19 — His Reading

This chapter is his. The instruments are the previous chapters'; the readings are his own. Each is filed verbatim in his words, tagged [H], with the documented seams marked beside them — never debated, never applied to his personal values. What his readings mean for his health is Kelsey Meyer's Monday.

**Simply put:** Everything so far was the record's — coats, drugs, labs. This chapter is Douglas's own reading of it: how he looks at the numbers, what he thinks they say about fungus versus bacteria, and how he built his knowledge. His words, his rules, filed exactly. No one else's verdict is added — least of all about his health.

### The equilibrium — his method, his words [H]

*"The Mirsa-Namrsa for each serotype is the equilibrium that forces me to know if the balance of sickness between available serotypes to be tested if it's more fungal than bacterial and it's a balance between both. And I can look at that test result and figure that out based upon that. And I don't know if there's any other physicians on earth that are able to do that based upon just looking at it like I can."*

(Filed reading: "Mirsa-Namrsa" = **MRSA/non-MRSA**, his rendering.)

This is his method of reading, separated into its claims [H]:

1. **The equilibrium:** the MRSA/non-MRSA per serotype is the fulcrum by which he reads the fungal-vs-bacterial balance of sickness across the tested serotypes — more fungal than bacterial, or the balance between both.
2. **His self-assessment:** he can look at the test result and figure that out — and he doesn't know if any other physician on earth can do it just by looking, like he can. Filed as his self-assessment: no endorsement, no dispute.

Chapter 17's table is the documented instrument he reads it from. The reading is his, his to make — this chapter makes no reading of it, and none of his personal values.

### His cross-referencing account — the root of the Full Tree [H]

His words (§165, filed Oct 17): the Mayo panel work was "enlightening" — by cross-referencing he found serotype links to **aspergillosis and other bacteria and fungi**. That cross-referencing is where he was building the Full Tree. His belief: that tree "would give medicine the best idea of what to do." [H]

His triangulation method, in his order, filed as his account of how he built the knowledge (§167, his words):

*"In order to find that knowledge, I used the panel information itself, plus I did in-depth PubMed searching, and I cross-referenced the 23 valiant vaccine to the 23 serotypes and then I would cross-link that information to the data of the vaccine and the data from the Mayo Clinic testing and then I went to Crest Laboratories to see what their information was and then I was looking at information from out of his country to the United Kingdom to piece the stuff together."*

Filed readings: "valiant" = **valent** (the 23-valent vaccine); "Crest Laboratories" = **Quest Laboratories**; "from out of his country to the United Kingdom" = looking outside this country, to the United Kingdom, to piece it together. His corrections open. The method in six steps: (1) the panel information itself — his Mayo PN23M; (2) in-depth PubMed searching; (3) cross-referencing the 23-valent vaccine to the 23 serotypes; (4) cross-linking the vaccine's data with the Mayo testing data; (5) Quest's information; (6) UK sources. Panel → literature → vaccine↔serotype → vaccine data ↔ Mayo data → Quest → UK [H]. His 46 cells — aspergillosis, bacteria, fungi per each of the 23 serotypes — are the intended contents of Chapter 15's empty map. His to dictate, his to evidence.

### The arbopanel in parallel — his framework, his words [H]

*"So take my Arbo panel in parallel that to each one of those sicknesses or viruses are tested against with azole class medicines to make them go away within your blood. Now, with Dr. Obeyed and I were discussing is that azole class drugs on their own for certain individuals aren't enough. And that's because there's still bacterial elements that are secondary. And that's why CBCs are important to measure the white blood cell count to get somebody on a medicine that they can tolerate."*

("Dr. Obeyed" = Dr. Obaid, his rendering.)

His framework, separated [H]:

1. **The parallel:** his arbopanel laid in parallel — each sickness/virus tested against azole-class medicines, to make them go away within the blood.
2. **His + Obaid's discussion:** azole-class drugs **on their own aren't enough for certain individuals** — because **secondary bacterial elements** still remain.
3. **The instrument:** **CBCs matter** — the white blood cell count guides getting somebody on a medicine they can tolerate.

Beside it, his sharper thesis [H]: *"The Arbo panel is the susceptibility for azole for each one of those. And not the 23, but the 23 is related to it. And azoles and their antibiotics are related to each stereotype in their treatment."* ("stereotype" = serotype.) Filed as his: the arbopanel IS the azole susceptibility; the 23 is related, not the same; azoles and their antibiotics relate to each serotype in their treatment. The honest seam [D]: susceptibility runs on cultured isolates, antibody panels on serum — the lab's architecture keeps them separate, and his "IS the susceptibility" is his elevation over it. His distinction (arbo ≠ 23, related) is filed as his framework. No treatment guidance is added here.


### The fossil record — vaccine → disease-name map [D + LOGIC]

The reverse-engineering ran the vaccines backward: the vaccines are named for the organism ("pneumococcal"), not for any disease. The disease names live one level down, attached to serotypes. Reversed outward [D]:

- **PCV7 (2000): 4, 6B, 9V, 14, 18C, 19F, 23F** → disease names: otitis media (6B, 19F, 23F, 14), meningitis (14, 18C, 4), carriage (6B, 19F, 23F), pneumonia (4, 9V). The children's set: ears, brains, and the nose they live in.
- **PCV13 additions (1, 5, 7F, 3, 6A, 19A)** → epidemic meningitis (1, 5), the persister (3), the replacement (19A), vaccine failure (3, 7F, 19A), adult pneumonia (3).
- **PCV15 additions (22F, 33F)** → adult IPD, pediatric replacement deaths.
- **PCV20 additions (8, 10A, 11A, 12F, 15B)** → adult severe IPD, outbreaks, pediatric severe IPD, the armed replacement (15B).
- **PCV21 (2024)** → adult severe disease + the orphan rescue (9N, 17F).
- **PPSV23 (1983)** → the archive: all of the above plus the never-conjugated (2, 20).

[LOGIC] — the fossil record. Chain: (a) each vaccine generation's serotype list is dated [D]; (b) each list was assembled in response to the failures of the previous one — 19A after PCV7, 33F after PCV13 [D]. Therefore: read the compositions in date order and you read the history of what frightened the field: 2000 = children's ears and meningitis; 2010 = the replacement and the persister; 2021 = adult invasive disease; 2024 = adult severe disease. The disease names moved first; the capsule numbers followed. The "disease names of vaccines" he asked for are this list — the vaccine is named for the bug, but its contents are named for the emergencies.

### The strep–staph–pneumonia triangle, reverse-engineered

**Documented edges [D]:** H2O2 warfare (pneumo→staph, SpxB); negative carriage association (aOR 0.53–0.67); PCV7 clearance doubled staph colonization in children; stable dual-species biofilms; dispersion modulation under viral conditions (pneumo disperses, staph inhibited — mBio 2017); flu-infected co-colonized mice → almost exclusively pneumococcal pneumonia; cocolonization rates rose post-PCV; throat co-carriage 7% during ARI with no negative interaction in the oropharynx.

[LOGIC] — the triangle's third corner is the syringe. Chain: (a) PCV7 cleared its seven serotypes [D]; (b) staph nasal colonization doubled in children [D]; (c) pneumo–staph cocolonization rates increased post-PCV [D]. Therefore: every pneumococcal vaccine is a staph intervention in reverse. The triangle isn't strep–staph–pneumonia — it's strep–staph–vaccine. Remove one corner's serotypes and the other corner expands into the room. His niche-thinking holds at the population level, documented.

[LOGIC] — the virus picks the winner. Chain: (a) dual biofilm + viral-like conditions → pneumococcus disperses, staph dispersal inhibited [D]; (b) flu-infected co-colonized mice developed almost exclusively pneumococcal pneumonia [D]. Therefore: in the co-colonized airway, influenza doesn't merely "open the door" — it selects which bacterium walks through. The post-flu pneumonia pathogen is decided at the biofilm, before the lung. "Secondary bacterial pneumonia" has a selection step nobody names.

[LOGIC] — eighteen empty cells. Chain: (a) only 5 of 23 serotypes have documented direct staph interaction [D]; (b) the other 18 inherit the species-level library [D]. Therefore: the triangle is documented at species resolution and empty at serotype resolution. The reverse-engineering maps the edges that exist and marks the empty cells as the work ahead — with 23F (strongest resistant biofilm, no staph data) as the most conspicuous absence and 14 (tall rough biofilm, staph-positive) as the shape to compare it against.


### The soy question — his claim, the record's answer [H, D, GAP]

His claim, his words: *"soy polysaccharide is part of the chemistry and is related to the saccharides of the different serotypes."* [H]

**The firm foothold [D]:** pneumococcal serotypes ARE defined by capsular polysaccharide chemistry — the capsule's sugar structure is the serotype. That part was never in doubt.

**Soy in the manufacturing [D]:** EP patent 2894225 (capsular polysaccharide production): serotypes cultivated "in a general medium such as a **soy-based medium**" — serotypes 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6A, 6B, 7F, 9N, 9V, 14, 18C, 19A, 19F, 22F, 23F, 33F named. PMC5625727 (pilot PCV-13): 13 serotypes fermented in "**customized soy peptone/yeast medium** recipes." US patents 20140099337 / 9981035 / 20150038685: "**each pneumococcal polysaccharide serotype is grown in a soy-based medium**," then purified, chemically activated, and conjugated to carrier protein. Frontiers 2024 (serotype 14 CPS optimization): feed medium with **200 g/L soy peptone** investigated.

**The honest boundary [GAP]:** soy peptone is a nitrogen source for growth — the record does not show soy saccharides incorporated into capsular polysaccharides; the bacterium synthesizes its own capsule from its own enzymes. Shared monomers (galactose, glucose, arabinose appear in both soy and various capsules) = shared bricks, different buildings. The "related" holds at the monomer level; anything stronger is [GAP] — his to evidence.

[LOGIC] — soy is upstream of every capsule. Chain: (a) every pneumococcal vaccine's polysaccharides are grown in soy-based medium [D]; (b) the purified product of that culture is the capsular polysaccharide [D]. Therefore: his "part of the chemistry" holds at the manufacturing level — soy broth is literally upstream of every capsule in every vial. The chemistry of the vaccine begins in soy.

**Simply put:** Douglas said soy is part of the serotype sugar chemistry. The record gives him this much: every vaccine's coat-sugars are grown in soy broth — soy is literally the first ingredient upstream of every vial. But soy broth is food for the germ, not a building block of the coat; the bacterium builds its own capsule from its own enzymes. Shared sugar bricks, different buildings. "Related" at the bricks — anything stronger is his to prove.

### The TB sidebar — a different kind of tree [D]

The record keeps a correction beside the serotype work: *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* has **no serotypes** — its tree is MTBC lineages L1–L8 [D]. And there is essentially one TB vaccine, not five: **BCG** (live attenuated *M. bovis*), made by many manufacturers as different **substrains** — Danish 1331, Tokyo 172-1, Pasteur 1173P2, Russian BCG-I, Moreau RDJ — all deriving from the 1924 Calmette–Guérin strain [D]. The substrains are used interchangeably with no conclusive evidence discriminating efficacy or safety between them [D]. BCG's protection is **cell-mediated, not antibody-mediated** — Th1 immunity, IFN-γ supercharging macrophages, granuloma formation — and the WHO (2011) recommended **against** commercial serodiagnostic antibody tests for TB as inaccurate [D]. Strong against severe childhood TB (~70–80%), highly variable against adult pulmonary TB (0–80% across trials) [D]. No licensed replacement as of Oct 2026 [D].

Why it sits in this chapter: his "Tuberculosis Pneumoniae" workup (his coinage, §187) pulls the 23 serotypes to the hub — and the record answers that TB's lineages and BCG's substrains are a different naming logic entirely. The serotype machinery doesn't transfer. Filed as contrast, not contradiction — his coinage stays his [H].

**Simply put:** TB doesn't have coat numbers at all — it has family lines instead. And its vaccine is really one old vaccine (BCG) made in five factory versions, none proven better than the others. Its protection lives in the T-cells, not the antibodies — which is why an antibody test for TB was officially discouraged. The pneumococcal coat-number system doesn't carry over to TB. That's the record's correction, filed beside his coinage without disturbing it.

### The readings, gathered

His readings sit together here as his, the way he reads:

- **The sputum** (Record One): *"To me that test tells me that I have streptococcus pneumoniae that's bacilli and coceae"* — bacilli cousin to anthrax pneumoniae, cocci related to Valley Fever; fungal and viral and bacterial together, hard to diagnose; maybe unnamed, maybe old [H].
- **The panel** (Record Two): his cross-referencing was "enlightening" — serotype links to aspergillosis and other bacteria and fungi, the root of his Full Tree, which "would give medicine the best idea of what to do" [H].
- **The arbopanel** (Record Three): the susceptibility in his framework; the parallel against azoles; azoles alone not enough where bacterial elements are secondary; CBCs to guide tolerability [H].
- **The equilibrium:** the MRSA/non-MRSA per serotype — the fulcrum by which he reads the fungal-vs-bacterial balance of sickness, by looking [H].

Each is his account of his own records — none argued, none applied. What they mean for him is Kelsey's Monday. The manuscript's half of his reading is this: file it exactly, tag it honestly, and leave the joining to him.

## Chapter 20 — The Full Tree, Condensed

His commission was the capstone of the October run, his own words [H — his commission, dictated-notes section 97]:

*"Make a list of viruses and their known companions, whether it's diseases by name and then if there are diseases by name, associated with those viruses, connect them to older names that are close that match or match exactly and then show the fungus, bacteria, pneumonia, or tuberculosis that's connected to each one of them."*

The tree he ordered has five levels, and every chain in it climbs the same ladder [D — the architecture is his commission; the contents are the record's]. **Level 1** is the virus itself, with its arboviral cousins noted alongside. **Level 2** is the known companions — the co-pathogens and associated microbes the record actually places with it [D]. **Level 3** is the diseases by name, the modern names [D]. **Level 4** is the old names — older names that are close, matching, or exact, from the namology record [D]. **Level 5** is the microbial anchor: the fungus, bacterium, pneumonia (serotype where the map has one), or tuberculosis connected to the chain [D — where the record reaches; GAP — where it doesn't].

**Simply put.** Think of the tree as a family tree, but for germs. At the top sits one virus. Below it: the germs it travels with, the diseases it causes, the old names those diseases used to wear, and — at the bottom — whether any fungus, bacterium, pneumonia germ, or tuberculosis germ stands documented at the end of the chain. Five rows. Every virus gets one. Where the record runs out, the branch stays empty on purpose — because an empty branch is honest, and a filled-in guess is not.

### The theses framing the tree

The tree's theses are his framing, and they are doing load-bearing work [H — all five]. Chase not just the pathogen but the pathogens it is *connected to* — the network, not the node (89). The rehearsal principle: the body mimics disease to know it first, so it can fight it later (90). Purposeful colonization: the body *keeps* bacteria, fungi, yeasts as preparedness; the colonists are part of pneumonia itself (91). Test pneumonia against the Mayo/Quest 23-serotype panels; partial relationships must be examined, never discarded, never upgraded (92). Immunity works through nucleic-acid relatedness between viral cousins — and **VNA**, Viral Nucleic Acid, names the shared element (94–95) [H — his coinage; the relatedness mechanism is documented under other names, [D]; his section-199 ruling settles it: VNA = Viral Nucleic Acid].

**Simply put.** Five rules from Douglas before the tree starts. One: don't chase one germ alone — chase the gang it runs with. Two: the body rehearses sickness to learn it, the way you rehearse a fire drill. Three: the germs living in you are there on purpose, kept as supplies. Four: when the record is partial, say it's partial — don't throw it out and don't pretend it's more. Five: immunity runs on relatedness, and the shared piece has a name: VNA.

### Part A — families WITH arboviral cousins, the full chains

**Flaviviridae (+ssRNA) — the textbook cousin set.** The kinship: single-ORF polyprotein, envelope E protein with the conserved fusion loop — the record's VNA for this family [D]. The ride varies (mosquito, tick, blood); the kinship doesn't.

- **Dengue virus 1–4** (mosquito, *Aedes*) [D]. L2: chikungunya virus — co-infection, shared *Aedes* vector [D]; Zika virus — sequential infection, antibody-dependent enhancement [D]. L3: dengue fever; dengue hemorrhagic fever; dengue shock syndrome [D]. L4: **"breakbone fever"** — exact old name, named for the pain [D]. L5: [GAP — no documented fungus/bacterium/pneumonia/TB link at 88%].
- **Zika virus** (mosquito, *Aedes*) [D]. L2: dengue — cross-reactive antibodies, ADE both directions [D]. L3: Zika fever; congenital Zika syndrome (microcephaly) [D]. L4: none documented (described 1947, no old name) [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Yellow fever virus** (mosquito) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: yellow fever [D]. L4: **"yellow jack"** · **"vomito negro"** (black vomit) [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **West Nile virus** (mosquito, *Culex*; birds the reservoir) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: West Nile fever; West Nile neuroinvasive disease [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Japanese encephalitis virus** (mosquito) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Japanese encephalitis [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **St. Louis encephalitis virus** (mosquito) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: St. Louis encephalitis [D]. L4/L5: [GAP].
- **Tick-borne encephalitis virus** (tick) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: tick-borne encephalitis [D]. L4: "Central European encephalitis" (old regional) [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Powassan virus** (tick) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Powassan encephalitis [D]. L4/L5: [GAP].
- **Hepatitis C virus** (blood/needles — the NON-arboviral cousin, genus *Hepacivirus*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: hepatitis C; chronic hepatitis; cirrhosis; hepatocellular carcinoma [D]. L4: **"non-A, non-B hepatitis"** — exact old name, pre-1989 [D]. L5: [GAP].

**Simply put.** Nine cousins, one family. Seven ride mosquitoes or ticks; one rides blood. That blood-rider — hepatitis C — is the proof of the doctrine: the family is about the genome, not the vehicle. You can tell the family is real because the lab tests get confused across it: dengue's test trips over Zika, and West Nile's own test label warns that a yellow-fever shot can make it read positive. The cousins answer together.

**Togaviridae (+ssRNA).** The kinship: one genus rides insects (*Alphavirus*), the other rides air (*Rubivirus* — rubella alone). The ride diverges; the kinship holds [H→D].

- **Chikungunya virus** (mosquito, *Aedes*; *Alphavirus*) [D]. L2: dengue — co-infection, shared vector [D]. L3: chikungunya fever; chronic inflammatory arthralgia (months–years) [D]. L4: "chikungunya" itself — Makonde for **"that which bends up"**; the name *is* the showing [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Eastern / Western / Venezuelan equine encephalitis viruses** (mosquito; *Alphavirus*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: EEE / WEE / VEE — equine encephalitides, human spillover [D]. L4: none beyond the geographic tags [D]. L5: [GAP]. All three are U.S. select agents [D].
- **Rubella virus** (respiratory droplets — the NON-arboviral cousin, genus *Rubivirus*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: rubella; congenital rubella syndrome [D]. L4: **"German measles"** — geographic; German physicians distinguished it from measles [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Further alphavirus cousins (compact):** Sindbis · Ross River · Mayaro · O'nyong-nyong — L3: febrile arthralgia syndromes [D] · L4/L5: [GAP].

**Bunyavirales, order (−ssRNA, segmented) — his panel's order.** The kinship: three genome segments — small/medium/large: nucleocapsid, glycoproteins, polymerase [D]. His arbopanel viruses live here.

- **La Crosse virus** ⭐ (mosquito, *Aedes triseriatus*; *Orthobunyavirus*, California serogroup) [D]. L2: [GAP — no documented co-pathogen companions at 88%]. L3: La Crosse encephalitis [D]. L4: **"California encephalitis"** — close old name: early cases were attributed to California encephalitis virus before La Crosse's 1960s isolation [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Jamestown Canyon virus** ⭐ (mosquito, many *Aedes* species; same serogroup) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Jamestown Canyon virus disease [D]. L4: "California encephalitis" — close (serogroup attribution) [D]; **"Jamestown Canyon Creek Fever"** — his rendering [H]. L5: [GAP]. Cross-link [H]: his conjoined-symptomology thesis pairs JCV disease with coccidioidomycosis (valley fever) — *"truly symptomatically the same things with different names"* [H].
- **Rift Valley fever virus** (mosquito; *Phlebovirus*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Rift Valley fever [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Crimean-Congo hemorrhagic fever virus** (tick; *Orthonairovirus*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Crimean-Congo hemorrhagic fever [D]. L4: **"Crimean hemorrhagic fever"** + **"Congo virus"** — two names recognized as one virus, 1969 (exact match by merger) [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Sin Nombre virus** (rodents, aerosolized excreta — the NON-arboviral cousin; *Hantaviridae*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: hantavirus pulmonary syndrome [D]. L4: **"Four Corners illness"** — exact old name, 1993 [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Oropouche virus** (biting midges, *Culicoides*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Oropouche fever [D]. L4/L5: [GAP].
- **Further cousins (compact):** California encephalitis virus (the serogroup namesake) · Snowshoe hare, trivittatus, Keystone, Inkoo viruses — L3: California-serogroup encephalitides [D] · L4: "California encephalitis" [D] · L5: [GAP].

**Reoviridae (dsRNA, segmented).**

- **Colorado tick fever virus** (tick, *Dermacentor andersoni*; *Coltivirus* — 12 segments, ~29,000 bp, among the largest arbovirus genomes) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: Colorado tick fever [D]. L4: **"mountain tick fever"** — old regional [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Rotavirus** (fecal–oral — the NON-arboviral cousin; *Sedoreovirinae*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: rotaviral gastroenteritis [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Eyach virus** (tick; *Coltivirus* — CTFV's European cousin, serologically related) [D]. L3: febrile illness [D]. L4/L5: [GAP].

**Rhabdoviridae (−ssRNA)** — the family that holds the most feared non-vector virus and genuine insect-borne members side by side; kinship ignoring the ride [H→D].

- **Rabies virus** (animal bite/saliva — the NON-arboviral cousin; *Lyssavirus*) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: rabies [D]. L4: **"hydrophobia"** — exact old name, centuries of use [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Vesicular stomatitis virus** (sandflies/blackflies/mosquitoes; *Vesiculovirus* — the arboviral cousin) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: vesicular stomatitis (livestock; mild human febrile illness) [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Chandipura virus** (sandfly; *Vesiculovirus*) [D]. L3: encephalitis (India) [D]. L4/L5: [GAP].

**Orthomyxoviridae (−ssRNA, segmented) — the flu's family.**

- **Influenza A virus** (respiratory droplets — the NON-arboviral cousin; *Alphainfluenzavirus*) [D]. L2: *Streptococcus pneumoniae* — THE classic synergy: ~100-fold susceptibility post-flu; neuraminidase exposes receptors; serotypes **2** (D39 kinetics model), **3** (A66.1 vaccine failure), **4** (0→79% mortality), **19F** (0→63% mortality) [D — serotype map]; *Staphylococcus aureus* — post-flu necrotizing pneumonia [D]; *Haemophilus influenzae* [D]. L3: influenza (flu); post-influenza pneumococcal pneumonia; post-influenza staphylococcal pneumonia [D]. L4: **"la grippe"** — the French old name ("the seizure") [D]; "influenza" itself — Italian, *influence of the stars* [D]. L5: **pneumonia — serotypes 2, 3, 4, 19F** [D — serotype map] · ***S. aureus*** [D] · TB: [GAP].
- **Influenza B / C viruses** (respiratory droplets) [D]. L2: [GAP at 88%]. L3: influenza (flu) [D]. L4: "la grippe" [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Thogoto / Dhori / Bourbon viruses** (ticks; *Thogotovirus* — the ARBOVIRAL cousins of flu) [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: febrile illness; Bourbon — fatal human case, 2014 [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: [GAP].

**Simply put.** Flu is the one virus in this chapter whose chain reaches all the way to the bottom of the tree. Flu → the pneumonia germ (four named serotypes) → pneumonia. And flu's own cousins ride ticks, not coughs. Same family, different delivery. The tree says: follow the genome, not the vehicle.

### Part B — families with NO arboviral members, the chains

No cousins on the arthropod side — the ride is direct contact, air, gut, sex, or birth, and the kinship stays within those rides [D]. The chains are shorter here, because L2 is mostly [GAP], but L4 — the old names — is rich, and a few chains reach the microbial anchors.

**Herpesviridae (dsDNA) — the lifelong guests** [D]. Large dsDNA, latency for life — the family that never leaves.

- **Herpes simplex virus 1** [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: cold sores / fever blisters [D]. L4: "herpes" — Greek, *creeping* (the ancient name kept) [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Varicella-zoster virus** [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: chickenpox (first meeting); shingles (the return, decades later) [D]. L4: **"shingles"** ← Latin *cingulum* (belt) — the same belt image as *zoster* (girdle), arrived through a different door [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Epstein-Barr virus** [D]. L2: *Plasmodium falciparum* (malaria) — co-factor in endemic Burkitt lymphoma [D]. L3: infectious mononucleosis [D]. L4: **"kissing disease"** · **"mono"** — the American names [D]. L5: [GAP — no fungus/bacterium/pneumonia/TB link at 88%].
- **Cytomegalovirus** [D]. L2: [GAP]. L3: CMV infection; congenital CMV [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: ***Aspergillus fumigatus*** — CMV infection/reactivation is a documented risk factor for invasive pulmonary aspergillosis in transplant/hematology patients [D — transplant literature; context-bounded, not general-population].
- **HHV-6/7/8 (compact):** HHV-6/7 — L3: roseola (**"sixth disease"** [D]) · HHV-8 — L3: Kaposi sarcoma (with HIV) [D] · L4: none · L5: [GAP].

**Adenoviridae (dsDNA) · Papillomaviridae (dsDNA) · Polyomaviridae (dsDNA) · Parvoviridae (ssDNA)** [D].

- **Adenoviruses.** L2: [GAP]. L3: pharyngitis, conjunctivitis (**"pinkeye"** [D]), pneumonia (barracks outbreaks) [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].
- **Human papillomaviruses.** L2: [GAP]. L3: cervical/anal/oropharyngeal cancers; genital warts [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].
- **Parvovirus B19.** L2: [GAP]. L3: erythema infectiosum [D]. L4: **"fifth disease"** — fifth of the six childhood exanthems [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **JC / BK (compact):** JC — L3: PML (progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy, immunosuppression) [D]; BK — L3: BK nephropathy (transplant) [D]; L4: none; L5: [GAP].

**Poxviridae (dsDNA) — the eradicated and the returned** [D].

- **Variola virus.** L2: [GAP]. L3: smallpox [D]. L4: **"smallpox"** distinguished from the **"great pox"** (syphilis) — the old pair [D]; Latin *variola* from *varius*, spotted [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Mpox virus (compact).** L3: mpox [D]. L4: "monkeypox" (renamed 2022 — the stigma correction) [D]. L5: [GAP].

**Picornaviridae (+ssRNA) — the small ones** [D].

- **Poliovirus.** L2: [GAP]. L3: poliomyelitis [D]. L4: **"infantile paralysis"** [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Rhinoviruses.** L2: *S. pneumoniae* — asymptomatic rhinovirus infection increases pneumococcal acquisition [D — serotype map]. L3: common cold (**"coryza"** — Greek *koryza*, head cold [D]). L4: **"coryza"** — the medical Latin that fell out of use [D]. L5: **pneumonia — *S. pneumoniae* (species level; no serotype-specific rhinovirus data)** [D — serotype map].
- **Hepatitis A virus.** L2: [GAP]. L3: hepatitis A [D]. L4: **"infectious hepatitis"** — old (vs. "serum hepatitis") [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Coxsackie / echo enteroviruses (compact).** L3: hand-foot-and-mouth; viral meningitis; myocarditis [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].

**Coronaviridae (+ssRNA) — the crowned ones** [D].

- **SARS-CoV-2.** L2: *S. pneumoniae*, *S. aureus* — secondary bacterial co-infections documented in hospitalized COVID-19 [D]. L3: COVID-19 [D]. L4: none (novel, 2019) [D]. L5: **pneumonia — *S. pneumoniae* (species level)** [D] · ***S. aureus*** [D].
- **SARS-CoV · MERS-CoV · seasonal coronaviruses (compact).** L3: SARS / MERS / common colds [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].

**Paramyxoviridae (−ssRNA)** [D].

- **Measles virus** (rubeola). L2: *S. pneumoniae* and other bacteria — measles predisposes to secondary bacterial pneumonia/otitis [D]. L3: measles [D]. L4: **"morbilli"** — old Latin [D]; "rubeola" = "little red one" [D]. L5: **pneumonia — *S. pneumoniae* (species level)** [D].
- **Mumps orthorubulavirus (compact).** L3: mumps [D]. L4: "mump" — 16th-century English, a grimace (named for the swollen face) [D]. L5: [GAP].

**Pneumoviridae (−ssRNA)** [D].

- **Respiratory syncytial virus.** L2: *S. pneumoniae* — RSV increases pneumococcal acquisition, density (9 days), and nasal shedding [D — serotype map]. L3: bronchiolitis; viral pneumonia [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: **pneumonia — *S. pneumoniae* (species level; no serotype-specific RSV data)** [D — serotype map].
- **Human metapneumovirus.** L2: *S. pneumoniae* **serotype 19A** + *Mycoplasma pneumoniae* — fatal pediatric triple detection [D — serotype map]. L3: hMPV respiratory illness [D]. L4: none documented [D]. L5: **pneumonia — serotype 19A** [D — serotype map].

**Retroviridae (+ssRNA → DNA) — the reverse writers** [D]. They write themselves into DNA via reverse transcriptase, integrating into the host genome.

- **HIV-1 / HIV-2.** L2: *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* — THE documented virus–bacterium co-infection (HIV fuels TB reactivation; TB accelerates HIV) [D]; *Cryptococcus neoformans* — cryptococcal meningitis [D]; *Candida* spp. — oropharyngeal candidiasis [D]; *S. pneumoniae* — invasive pneumococcal disease [D]. L3: AIDS [D]. L4: **"GRID"** (gay-related immune deficiency) — exact old name, 1982 [D]; **"slim disease"** — the African epidemic name [D]. L5: **tuberculosis** [D] · ***Cryptococcus*** [D] · ***Candida*** [D] · **pneumonia — *S. pneumoniae* (species level)** [D].
- **HTLV-1/2 (compact).** L3: adult T-cell leukemia/lymphoma; tropical spastic paraparesis [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].

**Hepadnaviridae (dsDNA-RT) · Hepeviridae (+ssRNA)** [D].

- **Hepatitis B virus.** L2: [GAP]. L3: hepatitis B [D]. L4: **"serum hepatitis"** — old, the transfusion name [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Hepatitis E virus.** L2: [GAP]. L3: hepatitis E [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].

**Other −ssRNA families (compact)** [D].

- **Lassa virus / LCMV** (rodents — NOT arthropod; *Arenaviridae*). L3: Lassa fever; lymphocytic choriomeningitis [D]. L4: none documented. L5: [GAP].
- **Ebola / Marburg viruses** (*Filoviridae* — arthropod involvement unproven). L3: Ebola/Marburg virus disease [D]. L4: **"Ebola hemorrhagic fever"** (earlier name, exact) [D]. L5: [GAP].
- **Borna disease virus** (*Bornaviridae*). L3: Borna disease (animals; rare human encephalitis) [D]. L4: none. L5: [GAP].

**Caliciviridae (+ssRNA)** [D].

- **Norovirus.** L2: [GAP]. L3: viral gastroenteritis [D]. L4: **"Norwalk virus"** — exact old name, the prototype strain, 1968 [D]. L5: [GAP].

**Simply put.** Out of roughly forty-five virus chains, most end at Level 5 with an empty slot. That's not a failure — that's the honest shape of the record. The viruses whose chains DO reach the bottom are a short list: flu, HIV, RSV, rhinovirus, measles, SARS-CoV-2, human metapneumovirus, CMV. Everything else is waiting on somebody to look.

### Convergence — the chains that reach the triad

His thesis (89): almost all things connect back to aspergillosis · pneumococcus · tuberculosis [H]. These are the chains where the record actually arrives there [D].

**Chains terminating in TUBERCULOSIS** [D]. And tuberculosis has the deepest old-name constellation in medicine [D]: **phthisis** (Hippocrates) · **consumption** · the **white plague** · the **king's evil / struma** — scrofula, the neck nodes, believed a different disease until Koch in 1882 · **lupus vulgaris**, skin TB · **tabes mesenterica**, the abdominal nodes · **Pott's disease**, the spine [D].

- HIV-1 → *M. tuberculosis* → AIDS/TB co-infection: virus → bacterium, both directions documented [D]. Old names on the route: "GRID," "slim disease" (HIV) [D] · phthisis, consumption, white plague (TB) [D].
- *S. pneumoniae* → *M. tuberculosis*: the vaccine-probe leg — −43.4% TB hospitalization with PCV9 [D]. Old name on the route: consumption [D].
- TB → *Aspergillus* (cavity) → back to TB: TB hollows the lung; the fungus moves into the hollow; the pair re-presents as "consumption" — wearing the oldest name on the oldest disease [D].

**Chains terminating in PNEUMONIA (*S. pneumoniae*)** [D].

| Chain | Route | Serotype (L5) |
|---|---|---|
| Influenza A → pneumo | ~100-fold susceptibility; neuraminidase exposes receptors | **2, 3, 4, 19F** [D] |
| RSV → pneumo | acquisition + density + shedding | species level [D] |
| Rhinovirus → pneumo | acquisition | species level [D] |
| hMPV → pneumo | fatal triple with *M. pneumoniae* | **19A** [D] |
| Measles → pneumo | secondary bacterial pneumonia | species level [D] |
| SARS-CoV-2 → pneumo | secondary bacterial co-infection | species level [D] |
| *S. aureus* ↔ pneumo | H₂O₂ warfare; PCV7 clearance doubled staph carriage | **3, 14, 18C, 19F, 19A** (biofilm five) [D] |

**Chains terminating in FUNGUS** [D].

| Chain | Route |
|---|---|
| CMV → *Aspergillus fumigatus* | CMV infection/reactivation → invasive pulmonary aspergillosis risk (transplant/hematology) [D, context-bounded] |
| *S. pneumoniae* → *A. fumigatus* | H₂O₂ shreds fungal biofilm [D, in vitro] |
| *A. fumigatus* → *S. pneumoniae* | gliotoxin inhibits pneumo, synergizes with cefotaxime/vancomycin [D, in vitro] |
| TB → *Aspergillus* | the cavity is the house: 14–22% aspergillomas (British cohort); ~1.2M post-TB CPA cases [D] |
| HIV → *Cryptococcus* | cryptococcal meningitis [D] |
| HIV → *Candida* | oropharyngeal candidiasis [D] |

**The simplest full-tree path** (his 88% bar, all [D]): **Influenza A → *S. pneumoniae* serotype 3 → pneumonia → (TB-damaged lung) → *Aspergillus* colonization.** Virus opens the door (flu → 100-fold susceptibility), the thickest-capsule serotype walks through (3/A66.1 — the one flu defeats vaccine protection against), the damaged architecture admits the fungus (TB cavity → aspergilloma). Four levels, one ruined building, every step measured.

**Simply put.** Picture a house. The flu blows the front door off. The thickest-armed pneumonia germ walks in. Where the walls were already knocked out by old tuberculosis, the fungus moves into the empty rooms. Nobody had to invent this — the record documents every step.

**The most complex full-tree path** (all [D]): **RSV → pneumococcal acquisition (species) → carriage (6B/19F/23F specialists) → influenza A superinfection → serotype 4 invasion (0→79% mortality) → TB co-infection (either direction, −43.4% with PCV9) → cavitation (20–40%) → *Aspergillus* colonization (22% at 3 years) → gliotoxin vs. H₂O₂ chemical war → HIV/TB-style triple states.** Nine links, never leaving the literature.

The tree's verdict, his way: chase the network, not the node — and where the record ends, the gaps are his to fill [H — his method].

### BUILDER'S EXTRA (labeled — mine, not his)

*Per his standing rule, one completeness addition of my own judgment, clearly marked as mine. The builder's extra for this chapter: the tree above runs virus-down. The record also wants to be read anchor-up — the clinician's question is "what leads HERE?" So, the reverse index:*

- ***Mycobacterium tuberculosis*** ← HIV-1 · *S. pneumoniae* (species) · *Aspergillus* (via cavity, reverse) · [GAP: no virus reaches TB directly at 88%]
- ***Streptococcus pneumoniae* (species)** ← influenza A · RSV · rhinovirus · measles · SARS-CoV-2 · *S. aureus* · *H. influenzae* · *M. catarrhalis* · *M. tuberculosis*
- **Serotype 3** ← influenza A (vaccine failure) · *S. aureus* (biofilm five) · [H→D] *Aspergillus* (capsule × H₂O₂, untested)
- **Serotype 19A** ← hMPV (+ *M. pneumoniae*) · *S. aureus* (biofilm five)
- **Serotypes 2, 4, 19F** ← influenza A
- ***Aspergillus fumigatus*** ← TB (cavity) · CMV (transplant) · *S. pneumoniae* (H₂O₂, in vitro)
- ***Staphylococcus aureus*** ← influenza A · SARS-CoV-2 · *S. pneumoniae* (H₂O₂ war) · PCV7 clearance (niche reshuffle)
- ***Cryptococcus* / *Candida*** ← HIV-1

*The forward tree answers "where does this virus lead?" The reverse index answers the clinician's question — "what leads HERE?" — which is how his section-55 doctrine gets used: start at the showing (the anchor), cross-link backwards to the cause. The tree now works in both directions. Mine, not his.*
