## Chapter 21 — The Cousins

His thesis, his words [H — his thesis, dictated-notes sections 94–95]:

*"Please make me a list of known human viruses and parallel arboviral viruses that may by one way or another scientifically be cousins or related by DNA for the RNA and DNA must somehow be connected or related for the reactions to happen between species. And that's how immunity actually works."*

His coinage: **VNA — Viral Nucleic Acid** [H — his coinage]. The shared, related nucleic-acid element across viral cousins: the kinship molecule. Where his thesis says the RNA and DNA of related viruses "must somehow be connected or related for the reactions to happen between species," VNA names the connection — the common nucleic-acid thread running through the cousin viruses, the thing the immune system is actually recognizing when it reacts across species [H — his coinage; the mechanism documented under other names, [D]]. Filed with the note: "NA" reads as *nucleic acid* — the alternative immunological expansion, Viral Neutralizing Antibody, stands one step downstream: shared VNA → cross-reactive neutralizing antibodies. His correction settles it [H — his ruling, section 199].

The doctrine comes first, because it governs everything in this chapter [D]: **"arbovirus" is an ecological word, not a taxonomic one** — it means *arthropod-borne*, the **ride**, not the **kinship**. A mosquito-borne flavivirus's cousins are other flaviviruses — including a tick-borne one and a blood-borne one — not other mosquito-borne viruses from other families. Relatedness is in the genome; the vector is just the delivery.

**Simply put.** Two words do all the work here: the *ride* and the *kinship*. The ride is the vehicle — the mosquito, the tick, the cough, the blood. The kinship is the family — the shared genome, the DNA and RNA that make two viruses cousins. Cousins can take different rides. A mosquito virus's closest cousin might ride a cough. Never mix up the vehicle with the family.

### The six kinship houses

Six families hold arboviral cousins — six kinship houses with tenants on both the insect and the non-insect sides [D]. Each house is named by its genome plan, and each has its own VNA — the shared element the cousins keep.

**1. Flaviviridae (+ssRNA) — the textbook cousin set** [D]. Kinship: single-ORF polyprotein, envelope E protein with the conserved fusion loop — the record's VNA for this family [D]. The fusion loop is the shared element behind both cross-neutralization *and* antibody-dependent enhancement — the family answering together, for better and for worse. Dengue 1–4, Zika, yellow fever, West Nile, Japanese encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, Powassan, tick-borne encephalitis — and the non-arboviral cousin, **hepatitis C**, blood-borne, same family, different ride [D].

**2. Togaviridae (+ssRNA)** [D]. Kinship: the family splits clean — one genus rides insects (*Alphavirus*: chikungunya, EEE, WEE, VEE, Sindbis, Ross River, Mayaro, O'nyong-nyong), the other rides air (*Rubivirus* — rubella alone, its sole member). The ride diverged; the kinship held [H→D — his reading, on documented taxonomy]. Rubella rides respiratory droplets, no vector at all [D].

**3. Bunyavirales, order (−ssRNA, segmented)** [D]. The order containing his arbopanel viruses. Kinship: three genome segments — small/medium/large: nucleocapsid, glycoproteins, polymerase [D]. His own panel viruses, **La Crosse and Jamestown Canyon** (California serogroup, mosquito-borne) — and the non-arboviral cousins, the **hantaviruses**, riding aerosolized rodent excreta instead of a mosquito [D]. California serogroup members cross-react serologically — up to 23% of Jamestown Canyon patients show La Crosse cross-reactivity — which is why a positive IgM demands confirmatory plaque-reduction neutralization testing [D]. His panel lives inside this cross-reactivity.

**4. Reoviridae (dsRNA, segmented)** [D]. Kinship: the segmented double-stranded plan. **Colorado tick fever virus** — 12 segments, ~29,000 bp, among the largest arbovirus genomes — and **rotavirus**, same segmented kinship, riding dirty water instead of a tick [D]. The nursery diarrhea virus and the Rocky Mountain tick virus are cousins. Eyach virus is CTFV's European cousin, serologically related [D].

**5. Rhabdoviridae (−ssRNA)** [D]. **Rabies virus** — animal bite/saliva, NOT arthropod, genus *Lyssavirus* — beside the vesicular stomatitis and Chandipura viruses on the insect side [D]. The family that contains the most feared non-vector virus and genuine insect-borne members side by side — kinship ignoring the ride, exactly as his thesis predicts [H→D].

**6. Orthomyxoviridae (−ssRNA, segmented)** [D]. Kinship: the segmented negative-strand plan; the polymerase shares the deeply conserved ANP32 host-factor dependence across a billion years of host divergence — kinship at the deepest level [D]. Influenza A, B, C — the non-arboviral cousins, riding coughs — beside the tick-borne **Thogotovirus** cousins: Thogoto, Dhori, **Bourbon** (discovered 2014, fatal Kansas index case; ~1% seropositivity in endemic Missouri) [D]. The flu's own cousins ride ticks.

**Simply put.** Six houses, and every house has tenants who arrived by different vehicles. The flu's cousins ride ticks. The yellow fever family's black sheep rides blood. The tick-fever virus's cousin rides nursery diarrhea. If you sorted viruses by who bit you, every one of these houses would split in half. Sorted by genome, they stay whole. That's the doctrine: the vehicle is just the delivery.

### The 16 panel viruses — every one related

Sixteen viruses sit on today's commercial and public-health test panels — West Nile, St. Louis encephalitis, EEE, WEE, La Crosse, Jamestown Canyon, dengue, Zika, chikungunya, yellow fever, Japanese encephalitis, Powassan, Colorado tick fever, tick-borne encephalitis, Bourbon, Heartland [D]. The cross-reference, done at his commission, returns one number: **16 of 16 have human-virus kin in the same family. Zero have none** [D]. Every panel virus sits inside a family crowded with human-virus cousins — which is exactly *why* arboviral serology cross-reacts. Mayo's own West Nile test label warns of false positives from dengue infection and from yellow fever, Japanese encephalitis, and dengue vaccination — the family answering together [D].

**Simply put.** Every virus on the test panels has family in the human world. Sixteen out of sixteen. Zero orphans. That is *why* the tests need a second, confirmatory test — because when the lab asks "is this West Nile?", the whole flavivirus family raises its hand. The confusion isn't a lab error. It's the kinship showing.

### The reverse cut — 17 families with no arboviral members at all

**17 human-virus families have no arboviral members at all** [D]: herpesviruses, adenoviruses, papillomaviruses, polyomaviruses, parvoviruses, poxviruses, picornaviruses, caliciviruses, coronaviruses, paramyxoviruses, pneumoviruses, filoviruses, arenaviruses, bornaviruses, retroviruses, hepadnaviruses, hepeviruses. In these families kinship never took the arthropod ride; the cousins travel by contact, air, water, blood, or birth [D]. These are where his thesis has no cross-ride test — no arboviral cousin exists to react against [D — absence as information, honestly filed].

**Simply put.** Turn the map around. Most virus families never took the insect ride at all. The cold viruses, the stomach-bug viruses, the herpes family, the coronaviruses, HIV — all travel by touch, air, water, blood, or birth. His thesis says the family is what matters, not the vehicle — and in these seventeen families, there's no insect cousin to test that against. Absence is information too.

### The ride map — vectors cross families, families cross vectors [D]

Read the panel list from the vector's side and the doctrine sharpens. **Mosquitoes** carry four families — flaviviruses, alphaviruses, orthobunyaviruses, phleboviruses. **Ticks** carry four — flaviviruses, coltiviruses, thogotoviruses, phleboviruses. No vector owns a family; no family owns a vector [D]. A laboratory organized by ride will always chase cross-reactions it didn't expect; a laboratory organized by family would expect them — because the cousins were the ones being tested all along [D — the record's pattern; the elevation is his].

**Simply put.** The mosquito is a bus, and four virus families ride it. The tick is another bus, also carrying four — a different four. No bus owns a family. So a lab organized by "what bit you" will always be surprised by the family answering. A lab organized by family would expect it. Expect the cousins — they're the ones being tested all along.

### The thesis tested — three ways

**Test 1 [D]: cross-reactivity tracks genetic relatedness — the flavivirus proof.** Dengue and Zika share ~60% nucleotide similarity. Most human anti-dengue monoclonal antibodies against the envelope protein also bind Zika; antibodies to the conserved **fusion-loop epitope** bind Zika but cannot neutralize it — and instead drive **antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE)** of Zika infection (Nat Immunol, PMID 27339099). Kinship → cross-reaction: his thesis, measured [D].

The complication is also measured, and the book carries it [D]: the same relatedness that enables cross-reaction can worsen disease — ADE, where sub-neutralizing cross-reactive antibodies aid viral entry via Fc receptors. Relatedness enables the reaction; it does not guarantee protection. This is the honest boundary of his thesis.

**Test 2 [D]: his own panel — the California serogroup proof.** Jamestown Canyon and La Crosse viruses — **his arbopanel viruses** — are California serogroup cousins. California serogroup viruses cross-react serologically; LACV cross-reactivity appears in up to 23% of JCV patients; a positive IgM requires confirmatory PRNT to rule out the cousins (PMC8182404). His test lives inside his thesis: the panel needs confirmation *because* the cousins are kin [D].

**Test 3 [D]: the documented names for his mechanism.** **Heterologous immunity** — memory of one pathogen shaping the response to another: LCMV and Pichinde virus (arenavirus cousins) share a cross-reactive epitope (NP205–212, 6 of 8 amino acids identical); in doubly-exposed mice the cross-reactive response dominates at 15–30% of CD8+ T cells. The immune system answering a cousin with the first cousin's memory [D]. **Original antigenic sin** (Francis, 1960) — first exposure imprints the response to later related antigens: described for influenza, dengue, rotavirus; the T-cell version documented alongside. The imprint *is* kinship recognition [D]. **The record's VNA** — conserved elements the immune system actually targets across cousins: the flavivirus EDE (envelope dimer epitope) bound by broadly neutralizing antibodies across dengue serotypes and Zika; the RdRp polymerase motifs conserved across RNA virus families; the fusion-loop epitope [D]. These are documented shared nucleic-acid/protein elements — his VNA, already in the literature under other names.

**Simply put.** Three confirmations. One: dengue's antibodies grab Zika — the closer the family, the louder the cross-reaction (and sometimes that backfires — the same grip can drag the virus into the cell). Two: his own two panel viruses trip over each other on the test — kinship, in his inbox. Three: medicine already has names for his idea — heterologous immunity, original antigenic sin, conserved epitopes. The mechanism is documented. His elevation — that this relatedness-reaction is not a quirk of immunity but its central operating principle, *"that's how immunity actually works"* — is his [H].

### The virology layer — the virus opens the door

Three findings from his vaccine/biome workup belong in this chapter, because they are the virology inside the cousin story [D].

**The lethal synergy: influenza × *Streptococcus pneumoniae*** [D]. In the 1918 pandemic, banked lung samples showed the vast majority had pneumococcal co-infection or secondary infection — one review puts ~95% of 1918 deaths down to flu–pneumo superinfection [D]. Three documented mechanisms [D]: (1) **the dead-cell anchor** — PspA acts as an adhesin specifically to *dead or dying* lung epithelial cells, missed for 30 years because researchers used healthy cell monolayers; flu kills the cells, pneumo anchors to the corpses (Cell Reports). (2) **immune sabotage** — influenza-induced type I interferon suppresses γδ-T-cell IL-17 and neutrophil attractants, crippling early bacterial clearance (J Clin Invest 2009). (3) **shared enzymes** — pneumococcal neuraminidases NanA/NanB support influenza replication; dual-acting neuraminidase inhibitors disrupt the synergy (PMC4800182). Post-viral bacterial pneumonia is the rule, not the exception: RSV and influenza both precede bacterial pneumonia; the major secondary invaders are *S. pneumoniae*, *S. aureus*, group B strep, and *H. influenzae* [D].

**Simply put.** The virus kicks the door down; the bacterium walks through. Flu kills lung cells, and the pneumonia germ grips the dead ones. Flu also jams the immune alarm so the cleanup crew never arrives. And the two share enzymes — they literally help each other replicate. In 1918 it wasn't just flu killing people; it was flu *opening* people for the pneumonia germ.

**The nasopharynx — the shared apartment** [D]. The nasopharynx is colonized in healthy people by *S. pneumoniae*, *H. influenzae*, *Moraxella catarrhalis*; the nasal cavity holds *Staphylococcus* spp. including *S. aureus* [D]. The interaction map is documented: pneumo × *S. aureus* = **antagonism** (pneumococcal hydrogen peroxide; immune-mediated competition); pneumo × *H. influenzae* = synergism and antagonism; pneumo × *M. catarrhalis* = both [D]. In children, pneumo, Hib, and *M. catarrhalis* positively associate with each other — and each is **negatively** associated with *S. aureus* [D]. And the kicker: PCV7 vaccination reduced pneumococcal colonization — and **staph colonization doubled** [D]. The biome is the unit the vaccines disturb.

**Simply put.** The nose is an apartment building, and the germs are tenants. The pneumonia germ and the staph germ are neighbors who fight — the pneumonia germ sprays hydrogen peroxide at the walls. When a vaccine evicted the pneumonia germ from the building, staph doubled. Evict one tenant, another takes the rooms. Nobody consults the landlord.

**The gut–lung axis** [D]. Gut and lung mucosa function as a single aerodigestive immune system; gut disruption changes airway immune tone [D]. Mechanism: bacterial short-chain fatty acids (e.g., butyrate) suppress IFN-γ and IL-17 T-cell function — the same IL-17 axis that clears pneumococcus from the upper tract [D]. The documented chain, stated straight: gut SCFAs suppress IL-17 → IL-17 is the documented clearance mechanism for pneumococcal colonization → gut dysbiosis can disarm the lung's anti-pneumo defense without the lung ever being touched [D; the clinical weight is GAP].

**Simply put.** The gut and the lung share an immune switchboard. The gut's bacteria make chemicals that turn down the lung's defenses against the pneumonia germ. You can weaken the lungs by upsetting the gut — without touching the lungs at all.

### The vaccine track — kept light, per his scope ruling

The deep vaccinology belongs in his future book, not this one [H — his standing rule]. One passage, his honesty habit applied. **The scoreboard:** pneumococcus — 5 licensed (PPSV23 1983, PCV13 2010, PCV15/PCV20 2021, PCV21 2024). Pneumonia's causes — 5 licensed (Hib, influenza, COVID-19, RSV, pertussis). TB — 1 licensed, BCG (1921), with M72/AS01E's Phase 2b ~50% efficacy and Phase 3 results expected 2028 [D]. And ***Staphylococcus aureus* — zero.** Three instructive failures: StaphVAX (efficacy faded 57%→26%), Merck V710 (halted — no efficacy and 5× mortality in infected vaccinees), Pfizer SA4Ag (discontinued for futility) [D]. The field's own conclusion: vaccinating against a *colonist* may need a different endpoint than vaccinating against an *invader* [D].

Across the ~50 viruses of the Grand Expansion, licensed vaccines exist for roughly twenty; the rest is the empty half of the table — herpes simplex, hepatitis C, Zika, HIV, West Nile [D]. The empty half is not a gap in the manuscript — it is a documented finding, and it is where his future book lives [D].

**The reversology spine, briefly** [H — his coinage, defined: the reverse-study of a finished thing — start with the product in the vial and interrogate it backwards: finished product → components → substrates → origins]. What the label says is [D]; what the label doesn't say is [GAP]. The spine of the 2010 dissection, kept light: the combination vaccines interrogated backwards — toxoids (bacterial fermentation), acellular pertussis pieces, yeast-brewed HBsAg (fungal factory), Vero-grown inactivated polio (monkey cells), PRP polysaccharide + carrier protein. **One syringe, four kingdoms: bacterial products, viral antigens, fungal-brewed protein, mammalian cell substrates** [D — all on the labels]. His bowl, in a vial. And twice on the 2010 list, **yeast is the factory**: HepB and Gardasil are brewed in *S. cerevisiae* — the only fungal *production* relationships on the list [D].

**Simply put.** Count the vaccines per germ and you can read medicine's relationship to it. Pneumonia germ: five. Tuberculosis: one, from 1921. Staph — the germ his thesis keeps circling: zero, and three expensive failures. Many vaccines means the "invader" playbook works. Zero means the "colonist" playbook is missing. Staph is the proof that the *paradigm*, not the effort, is the gap. And when you read a vaccine's label backwards — product to parts to what it was grown in — one syringe can hold four kingdoms: bacterial, viral, fungal, mammalian. The whole tree of life, in a vial.

The cousin verdict, tested three ways [H→D — his thesis standing on documented ground]: the record confirms the mechanism — immunity reacts across viral cousins in proportion to relatedness. What remains his: the elevation — that this relatedness-reaction is not a quirk of immunity but its central operating principle, *"that's how immunity actually works"* [H — his words].

## Chapter 22 — The Brain Census

His commission was a percentage, his words [H — his commission, dictated-notes section 179]:

*"When La Crosse encephalitis is considered part of an arbopanel, I imagine that we have to get to the point where encephalitis is part of all viruses if they're making you sick and your brain is always going to have some type of reaction of an inflammatory matter as part of the markers and showing the disease itself. And encephalitis affects the whole body just like aspergillosis and they're 101% connected and 100% connected. And I don't know what the computer can show percentage-wise of data that's available that would show a percentage of potential exactness of that. But I would like that study right now."*

His thesis, stated verbatim as his, the whole chapter's starting point [H — his, filed whole]:

*"Encephalitis is part of **all** viruses that make you sick and your brain is always going to have some type of reaction of an inflammatory matter as part of the markers and showing the disease itself. And encephalitis affects the whole body just like aspergillosis and they're **101% connected and 100% connected**."*

**Simply put.** Douglas starts from his own test panel. The panel tests for mosquito viruses, and one of them — La Crosse — is *named* for brain inflammation: La Crosse *encephalitis*. If the test's own vocabulary leads with the brain, why would the other viruses be exempt from it? His question: of all the viruses that make people sick, how many touch the brain? And he wanted a number.

### How the study was counted

The study counted **62 sickness-causing viruses** against the literature — the ~55 from the Full Tree plus seven additions where the CNS record is notable (JC virus, BK virus, HHV-6, enterovirus 71/D68, parechovirus, astrovirus, SARS-CoV, MERS-CoV) [D]. One row can be a group (dengue 1–4; EEE/WEE/VEE) where the CNS record is shared [D].

Three buckets [D]:

- **E — documented encephalitis** (brain-parenchyma inflammation documented in the literature — including where it is rare; rarity is noted per row, never hidden).
- **C — documented CNS involvement, but not encephalitis** (myelitis, meningitis-only, demyelination, neurocognitive, congenital brain injury, post-viral syndromes, peripheral neuropathy).
- **N — no documented CNS involvement** (nothing in the literature; "not established" rows carry a [GAP] where the literature is simply thin).

And the load-bearing wall: **encephalitis is not encephalopathy, is not meningitis, is not myelitis** — his pillars are different rooms of the same house, and the table says which room each virus has been found in [D — his four-pillar distinction, kept intact].

**Simply put.** Four rooms, one house. *Encephalitis* means the brain tissue itself is inflamed. *Encephalopathy* means the brain is reacting — confused, swollen — without the tissue being the battlefield. *Meningitis* is the brain's wrapping; *myelitis* is the spinal cord. The study sorted every virus into which room it has actually been found in — and where the record is thin, it says so.

### The family scoreboard — where the clean rows and the thin rows sit [D]

No virus family in the study is clean of the brain. But the families differ in how deep the record goes:

| Family | Rows | Encephalitis (E) | CNS, not encephalitis (C) | None / GAP (N) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Flaviviridae | 9 | 9 (yellow fever rare) | 0 | 0 |
| Togaviridae | 4 | 4 | 0 | 0 |
| Bunyavirales | 7 | 7 (CCHF, Sin Nombre rare) | 0 | 0 |
| Reoviridae | 3 | 2 | 0 | 1 (Eyach [GAP]) |
| Rhabdoviridae | 3 | 2 | 0 | 1 (VSV) |
| Orthomyxoviridae | 4 | 2 | 0 | 2 (influenza C; Thogoto group [GAP]) |
| Herpesviridae | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0 |
| Adeno / Papilloma / Parvo / Polyoma | 5 | 3 | 1 (JCV) | 1 (HPV) |
| Poxviridae | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 (variola, wild-type unestablished) |
| Picornaviridae | 7 | 7 | 0 | 0 |
| Coronaviridae | 3 | 3 (SARS/MERS rare) | 0 | 0 |
| Paramyxoviridae | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| Pneumoviridae | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| Retroviridae | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| Hepadnaviridae / Hepeviridae | 2 | 1 (HEV) | 1 (HBV) | 0 |
| Arenaviridae / Filoviridae / Bornaviridae | 3 | 1 (Lassa/LCMV) | 2 (Ebola/Marburg; partial) | 0 |
| Caliciviridae | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| Astroviridae | 1 | 1 (rare) | 0 | 0 |

**Simply put.** Every virus family has at least one member found in the brain's rooms. The mosquito-virus families — the ones on his panel — are 20-for-20 on encephalitis. The cold-and-stomach-bug family is 7-for-7 — the row his thesis would predict, and the literature confirms it. Six viruses have nothing documented. Three of those six are thin-literature rows: nobody looked.

### The percentages — his "percentage of potential exactness," from available data

| Bucket | Count | Share |
|---|---|---|
| **E — documented encephalitis** (including rare) | **53 of 62** | **85.5%** |
| **C — documented CNS involvement, not encephalitis** | 3 of 62 (JC virus, hepatitis B, Ebola/Marburg) | 4.8% |
| **E + C — any documented CNS involvement** | **56 of 62** | **90.3%** |
| **N — no documented CNS involvement** | 6 of 62 (Eyach, vesicular stomatitis, influenza C, Thogoto group, HPV, variola wild-type) | 9.7% |

**Simply put.** Fifty-three of sixty-two viruses have documented brain inflammation — 85.5%. Add the three with other brain involvement, and it's 56 of 62 — 90.3%. Six viruses have nothing documented: 9.7%. That is what the *record* says — what has been looked for and found. It is not 100%. And it is not his 101%. The gap between 85.5% and his number is where this book lives: the unlooked-for, the rare, the thin literature, and the question his thesis asks — whether the brain's inflammatory reaction is there in the rest, unmeasured.

### Where the brain shows up — the patterns in the table [D]

**1. The defining-encephalitis viruses (~20 rows).** The ones named by it or defined by it: Japanese encephalitis, tick-borne encephalitis, West Nile, La Crosse, EEE, rabies, herpes simplex encephalitis, measles (with its slow-burn SSPE, years later), polio, Chandipura [D]. For these, encephalitis isn't a complication — it's the disease's address.

**2. The encephalopathy viruses.** Influenza A and B, RSV, dengue — the brain reacts without the parenchyma being the battlefield [D]. Influenza-associated encephalopathy is well documented (ANE, 41 US cases 2023–2025, JAMA 2025) while true encephalitis is rare; RSV mostly encephalopathy [D]. His **encephalitis vs. encephalopathy** pillar distinction is the load-bearing wall of the whole study [D].

**3. The congenital face.** Zika, rubella, CMV, parvovirus B19 — the brain injured before birth or in infancy [D]. Encephalitis as a life sentence, not an episode.

**4. The slow-burn face.** Measles → SSPE, HIV → AIDS dementia, JC virus → PML: the brain's involvement measured in years [D]. His "viruses hang out" creed (§120) has its CNS chapter here [H].

**5. The rare-but-documented long tail.** Rhinovirus, astrovirus, BK virus, hepatitis A, adenoviruses — a handful of case reports each [D]. This is where his thesis is strongest as a *research program*: the tail exists because someone finally looked. Rotavirus encephalitis runs 2–6% of pediatric cases in a 2023 systematic review, with RNA found in CSF [D]. Norovirus encephalitis: a Japanese nationwide survey found 29 children, genome in CSF [D]. Oropouche's 2024 outbreak added 26 encephalitis and 15 meningoencephalitis cases in Cuba alone (PAHO/WHO) — newly, firmly documented [D].

**6. The honest six.** Eyach, vesicular stomatitis virus, influenza C, the Thogoto group, HPV, and wild-type variola — no documented CNS involvement [D]. Three of those six carry [GAP] for thin literature: Eyach, the Thogoto group, variola wild-type — not studied-and-absent, just unlooked-for [GAP]. VSV and influenza C and HPV are the studied rows with nothing found — the candidate true exceptions [D].

**Simply put.** The brain shows up in six ways. Some viruses are *named* for brain inflammation. Some make the brain react without invading it. Some injure the brain before birth. Some smolder for years. Some appear in a handful of case reports — because somebody finally looked. And six viruses show nothing: three because nobody looked, three because the looking came up empty.

### The honest seams

- **"Making you sick" is doing work** [D]. Asymptomatic carriage (anelloviruses, most HPV infections) doesn't enter the numerator — the study counts viruses with documented disease. His thesis says "if they're making you sick"; the study honors that boundary.
- **Rare is not absent, and rare is not common** [D]. Rhinovirus encephalitis is a handful of case reports against billions of infections; Japanese encephalitis encephalitis is the disease itself. The bucket is honest; the rows carry the rarity.
- **The looking-bias** [D/GAP]. Nobody runs encephalitis panels for Eyach or the Thogoto viruses because nobody suspects them; absence of documentation is partly absence of looking.
- **The vaccine rows are separated** [D]. Postvaccinial encephalomyelitis (smallpox) and vaccine-associated neurotropic disease (yellow fever) are filed under the *vaccine*, not the virus — his six tracks kept, vaccines ≠ viruses [H — his law, honored].

**Simply put.** Four honest lines around the number. One: the study only counts viruses that make people sick — silent carriers don't enter. Two: a handful of case reports and "it's the disease itself" are different things, and the rows say which. Three: nobody looks for brain inflammation in viruses nobody suspects — absence of evidence is partly absence of looking. Four: when the *vaccine* inflames the brain, that goes on the vaccine's row, not the virus's — the tracks stay separate.

### The verdict, tested

**What the literature gives him [D]:** 85.5% documented encephalitis, 90.3% any CNS involvement, no family clean of the brain — across 62 viruses.

**What the literature does not give him [D]:** the remaining 9.7% — and the intensity. "101%" is his; the computer returns 85.5%.

**The verdict his way (tested):** at 88% and above, his thesis holds as a *direction* — the brain is involved in the overwhelming majority of sickness-causing viruses, and the exceptions cluster where the literature is thinnest [H→D]. The step from 90.3% to 100% is his to evidence, row by row, and the table above is the map of exactly which rows need it.

## Chapter 23 — The Virome Creed

His creed, his words, verbatim [H — his dictation, section 120, filed as his]:

*"The virus hangs out until you get rid of the disease that underlies the virus. The virus doesn't either feast nor take or leave it just is part of the biome itself and you know viruses definitely are part of the process of everything including our bodies our bodies reactions to things and to the other to pests and the connection between those things."*

Three ideas, recorded separately per his rule [H]:

1. **The virus hangs out until you get rid of the disease that underlies the virus.** The causal arrow inverted — disease first, virus second; the virus persists as long as its underlying disease persists; resolve the disease, the virus goes. The virus is the showing, not the source.
2. **The virus neither feasts nor takes nor leaves — it just is, part of the biome.** Not parasite, not visitor — a resident member, present as a matter of course.
3. **Viruses are part of the process of everything** — our bodies, our bodies' reactions to things, reactions to pests, and **the connection between those things**. The virus as connective tissue: the link, not just the actor.

**Simply put.** Three sentences, plain. One: the virus stays until the sickness underneath it is gone — fix the sickness, the virus leaves. Two: the virus isn't a guest or a thief — it's a roommate, part of the household. Three: viruses are involved in everything the body does, everything it reacts to, everything it shares with pests — and the connections between all of those. The virus is the thread, not just the needle.

**Tested, honestly** [D/H→D]: the record holds the pattern he elevated. Opportunistic viruses recede when the underlying condition resolves — CMV and *Pneumocystis* quiet when HIV is treated and CD4 recovers; the clinical rule is *treat the underlying, the virus follows* [D]. The honest counter is filed beside it: the forward arrow also exists — primary pathogens causing disease in the healthy. Both arrows ride in this book [D/H — his elevation, honestly bounded]. "Neither feast nor take nor leave" is the commensal virome's plain-English definition: **anelloviruses** infect nearly every human for life without known disease — present, neither feasting nor leaving [D]. The permanent viral biome is documented; his sentence names it. And viruses as connectors — the mechanism is published: **bacteriophages move genes between bacteria (transduction)**, literally connecting organisms through shared genetic material; viral sequences stitched into host genomes connect species across evolutionary time [D].

### The dual role — sick and better

His theory runs beside the creed, his words [H — his dictation, section 181]:

*"My theory is that the viruses make you both sick and better and our thought process should probably be that the viruses are indicators and markers and they're part of the material of the clearing as well as the showing."*

The dual role: not only the bringer of sickness but part of the getting-better [H]. Viruses as indicators and markers — they show, they signal. Part of the material of the clearing *as well as* the showing — belonging to the disease's resolution and its presentation, both directions of the same material.

The "better" has documented legs [D]: latent herpesvirus infection protecting mice against bacterial pathogens (the virome as defense); viral interference, one virus blocking another; endogenous retroviruses built into mammalian birth itself — **syncytin**, a viral envelope gene captured into human DNA, literally making mammalian pregnancy work [D]. And the counter is filed beside it, honestly [D]: **measles immune amnesia** — a virus making you sicker later, the reverse direction, documented. The general principle is his, the legs are the record's [H→D].

**Simply put.** Viruses do two jobs. They make you sick — and they're part of making you better. They're signposts: they show where the sickness is. And they're part of the cleanup crew, too. The record backs the "better" half more than you'd expect: a captured virus gene holds the human placenta together, and a sleeping herpesvirus can protect a mouse from bacteria. But it also holds the counter: measles can erase immune memory. Both directions. The book carries both.

### The symbiotic counterparts — the body's answer

Then his mechanism, section 182, his words [H]:

*"Viruses contain parts of the sickness in their own body and our own body creates symbiotic counterparts to those viruses to create clearing mechanisms to get rid of the bulk and medications help that process to happen."*

The sickness-material rides within the virus; the body builds matching counterparts in symbiosis with the viral parts; the counterparts clear the bulk; medicines assist the process — helpers of the body's own clearing, not the clearing itself [H].

**The counterpart has a documented name: the antibody** [D]. The body reads the virus's parts (antigens) and builds matching counterparts — his "symbiotic counterparts" described in immunology's own vocabulary, clonal selection the documented mechanism [D]. "Medicines help that process happen" is standard medicine's own description of itself: antivirals stall replication while the immune system clears; supportive care holds the body up while it does the work [D]. The general principle is his; the anatomy is the record's [H→D].

**Simply put.** The virus carries pieces of the sickness inside it. The body builds a matching piece for each viral piece — a custom-fit key for every lock. That key has a documented name: the antibody. The body floods the field with keys, and the sickness clears. Medicines don't do the clearing — they help the body do it. Helpers, not heroes.

### The shared-illness theses — the creed's field reports

**Ticks on humans can be sick with the same thing**, his words [H — section 131]: *"it's not impossible that ticks on human beings could be sick with the same thing and we might share the viruses together."* The tick not just as vector but as co-sick — the same viruses moving between tick and human, shared. The documented ground: ticks carry what they transmit — Powassan, tick-borne encephalitis, Colorado tick fever, Bourbon, Thogoto/Dhori — the tick-borne rows of his cousin list, shared between tick and human virus by virus [D]; the general rule is his [H].

**Illness is shared across animals and different regions**, his words [H — section 173]: *"all animals that drink water most likely are sharing things like viruses, and the bacteria and the viruses that are in the water."* Cholera circles the water because all the drinkers share it. The record's company for this thesis has a name — **One Health**, the discipline holding human, animal, and environmental health as one shared system — and waterborne transmission is documented ground [D]; the elevation is his [H].

**We may be carrying the insect viruses themselves**, his words [H — section 175]: *"It's not impossible that we might just be carrying the insect viruses themselves. And our body, because of the RNA and DNA will react."* Not merely bitten, but carrying — and the body reacts to the RNA and DNA. The documented seam: transient carriage is documented — viremia after arthropod bites (dengue, Zika, West Nile all documented in blood post-bite); the virome carries many viruses asymptomatically and chronically (anelloviruses) [D]. The standing-carriage thesis — not transient viremia but carried — is his to evidence [H].

**The flow goes both ways**, his words [H — section 176]: *"the flow goes both ways. And it should be studied to see if these insects are carrying human viruses from sick people after they bite a sick person. And the study should be a hundred percent in both directions to get all the right answers."* The arbovirus cycle *is* vectors acquiring from hosts — a mosquito takes a blood meal from a viremic host and the virus replicates in the mosquito [D]; **xenodiagnosis** — lab-reared insects fed on patients, then tested — is the documented method wearing his proposal's name [D]; his extension beyond arboviruses, studied 100% bidirectionally, is his to evidence [H].

**Simply put.** Four field reports from the same creed. The tick isn't just a delivery truck — it can be sick with the same thing you are. Sickness crosses species at the watering hole — every drinker shares what's in the water. We may be carrying insect viruses ourselves, not just getting bitten. And the traffic runs both ways — the insect picks up our viruses when it bites us. Test the insect, not just the person. Both directions, a hundred percent.

### The coronavirus theses — kept whole [H]

His account [H — section 112]: *"I was taught that the human coronavirus that is part of our totality of our viral immune system is a symbiotic relationship to the immunoglobulins of our body."* He says he was *taught* this — source open, filed as his received teaching. The four endemic coronaviruses circulate continuously and reinfect everyone repeatedly across life — standing colonists, the documented picture [D]; calling the arrangement *symbiosis* (mutual benefit, not just tolerance) is his thesis laid over it [H].

His elevation [H — section 113]: *"the totality of all human viruses that are related to those protein chunks in our bodies are the ones that are our defenders."* **The human coronaviruses are our defenders** — the defensive arm of the virome, posted at the protein chunks. The job description exists in the record: **bacteriophages adhering to mucosal surfaces**, a non-host-derived immune barrier; **murine norovirus restoring gut immunity** in germ-free mice [D]. Putting the uniform on the coronavirus specifically is his thesis, his to evidence [H].

**Simply put.** Douglas was taught that the everyday coronaviruses — the cold-causers that never leave us — live in partnership with our antibodies. And his own step further: they're the virome's guards, posted at the body's protein depots. The record confirms the *job* exists — viruses guarding mucosal walls is published. Assigning that job to the coronavirus is his thesis, his to prove.

### One molecular proof — sialic acid

One molecular proof of the connection closes the chapter, from his pneumococcal parts cross-reference [D]: **sialic acid** — the strongest link in the related-materials file. It caps the N-glycans on viral envelope glycoproteins (influenza, SARS-CoV-2 spike, HIV gp120); influenza hemagglutinin binds it to enter cells; pneumococcal neuraminidase cleaves the same sialic acid to expose bacterial receptors — and the bacterium then *eats* the freed sialic acid [D]. One molecule: the virus's doorway, the bacterium's dinner bell.

A viral-neuraminidase inhibitor improved survival ~75% in flu–pneumococcus coinfected mice [D]; the literature now designs **dual inhibitors against viral and pneumococcal neuraminidases together**, on published structural and functional similarities between the two enzymes — two kingdoms, one substrate, one drug target [D]. His thesis — that the materials the parts react with are all related — is literal here, and confirmed at the three strongest nodes (sialic acid; the glycan camouflage both sides wear; phosphorylcholine binding a receptor the virus's inflammation put there), plausible at three more, unproven at four. That is **[H→D] overall with [D] anchors** — his seed, the record's soil, the bridge field growing.

**Simply put.** One molecule, proof of everything. Sialic acid sits on the outside of cells. The flu virus uses it as a doorknob to get in. The pneumonia germ cuts the same doorknob off, eats it, and uses the exposed spot to grip the cell. One doorknob, two germs, two kingdoms — and now the labs are designing one drug that jams both germs at the same doorknob. That's his whole thesis in a single molecule: the materials are all related.

### The creed as the part's spine

The virome creed is the spine of Part 5 — and it ties the chapters together [H — his; the connections documented where marked]. The tree (Chapter 20) is the creed's map: the virus hangs out *with its cousins and companions*, the network it rides in [D/H]. The cousins (Chapter 21) are the creed's mechanism: the shared nucleic-acid element — VNA — is *why* the virus hangs out, recognized across species because the kinship is in the genome [H→D]. The brain census (Chapter 22) is the creed's test: if the virus is part of everything, the brain — the body's most watched organ — should show it, and 90.3% is the record's answer so far [H→D]. And the creed is the part's thesis: **the virus hangs out until the underlying disease is gone; it neither feasts nor takes nor leaves; it is part of the process of everything — and the connection between those things** [H — his words].

**Simply put.** The four chapters are one argument. The tree maps where viruses live. The cousins explain *why* the body's reactions cross species lines. The brain census tests whether the brain is really involved in all of it — nine out of ten, so far. And the creed says what it all means: the virus isn't a visitor or a thief. It's part of the household. Fix what it's showing you, and it goes.

# PART 6 — THE BETWEEN

*Manuscript material — creative theoretical work, never medical advice. The doctrine below is a book's doctrine: what to look at first, and why the order of looking matters. Nothing here tells any person what test to order, what any result means, or what to do about any sickness. Testing decisions belong to the clinician; interpretation of his records belongs to his clinician.*

## Chapter 24 — The Between Doctrine

His statement, his words, the whole doctrine in one breath [H — his dictation, section 137, filed verbatim]:

*"Anything that mimics tuberculosis symptoms 101% equals pneumonia by 99 to 100 of 101% for certain. and encephalitis between all of those that are, and if they've actually been able to detect 100% of encephalitis and or encephalopathy through serological testing all pneumonias, tuberculoses are already known somehow."*

And his two trees, section 168, the doctrine's frame [H — his words]:

*"With medicine you have at least two trees, but the two trees are the doctor and the patient. and the things in between are the things that get in the way of that doctor and that patient getting better. And there's a path for both to get better together."*

**Simply put.** Two trees grow in every sickroom: the doctor's and the patient's. Between them stands whatever blocks them both from getting better — the sickness in the middle, and the blame, and the games. The book's job is to name the between, read it first, and let the two trees grow toward each other instead of apart.

### The equation: what mimics TB *is* pneumonia

Take the doctrine's first half seriously, because the record takes it seriously [D — the record's own confusion, honestly filed]. For a century, everything that wasted the lungs was charted as tuberculosis — consumption, phthisis, the white plague. The endemic mycoses wore TB's name for decades: histoplasmosis was routinely misdiagnosed as TB across the Ohio-Mississippi Valley; coccidioidomycosis still is, wherever it isn't looked for [D]. The mimics didn't just resemble TB — they *were* TB, in the charts, until the test said otherwise.

And community-acquired pneumonia is the presentation that brings the patient in — the great container [D]. Bacterial, viral, fungal, tuberculous: all of it arrives as pneumonia first. In the endemic Southwest, 15–30% of CAP is Valley Fever (his "Creek Fever," confirmed as coccidioidomycosis by his own ruling) [D], and a Mayo Phoenix study found 24% of it never tested for the fungus at all [D].

**Simply put.** The lungs show the same wreckage for different causes, and the charts have always called the wreckage by whichever test got run. The same cough, the same fever, the same x-ray shadow — three different names depending on which door the doctor walked through. His equation says: stop trusting the door. Trust the wreckage, then test everything that could have made it.

So the equation restates the record plainly [H→D — his reading, with documented legs]: the things that look like TB *are* the things that look like pneumonia — because they are the same patients, the same lungs, the same showing, sorted by whichever test got ordered. His 99–100 of 101% is the record's own number, rounded up by a man who trusts his eyes.

### The between, named and readable

The doctrine's second half names the middle: **encephalitis/encephalopathy** — the middle pillar of his four — sitting between the TB-mimics and the pneumonias, and the book reads it first [H — his].

And the between is not theoretical. It is readable — right now, in the blood [D]: arboviral IgM/IgM panels (Mayo's ARBOP covers California/La Crosse, St. Louis, Eastern and Western equine encephalitis viruses by immunofluorescence; Quest and other reference labs run equivalents), the California serogroup with its documented 23% cross-reactivity, PRNT for confirmation. He has lived this panel — his, his mother's — filed as his account [H].

The between touches both ends by traffic, not by poetry [D]: pneumococcal meningitis ↔ encephalitis; influenza → encephalitis; measles → SSPE; COVID → encephalitis. His own encephalitis-in-all-viruses study (built October 17) counted 53 of 62 sickness-causing viruses with documented encephalitis (85.5%), 56 of 62 with any documented CNS involvement (90.3%), and the arboviral families went 20-for-20 on encephalitis [D — his study's count]. The lung pathogens reach the brain as a matter of record.

**Simply put.** There's a middle thing — the brain's inflammation — that sits between the lung sicknesses. You can see it in a blood draw today, at any real lab. And the record already shows the lung bugs getting to the brain, over and over. So the middle isn't a guess. It's a test you can order.

### The inversion: middle→ends

Here is the doctrine's working move, and it is a move about *order* [H — his]: medicine currently works **ends→middle** — the pneumonia workup, then consider TB, then consider neuro involvement. His doctrine runs **middle→ends**: complete encephalitis/encephalopathy serology FIRST, and let it sort the pneumonias from the tuberculoses from the mimics.

The testable form, plainly stated [H — his doctrine; the design is the builder's formalization, labeled as such in Chapter 25]: run full arboviral panels on community-acquired pneumonia cohorts in endemic regions, and measure how much of the "unknown pneumonia" and "presumed TB" burden resolves. The literature says the unknown-etiology fraction of CAP is 30–60% — the failure to detect a pathogen is the literature's most constant finding [D]. His Valley Fever numbers (the 24% never tested) say the burden is large; his doctrine says the between is where to look.

The trial does not run at his 101% — it runs at 80% power and 95% confidence like every other honest trial [D]. The man may be certain; the numbers must earn it. That separation is the manuscript's integrity [H — his idiom, the builder's method].

### The between's other occupants: blame and the games

The two trees have more than sickness standing between them [H — his doctrines, sections 177–178]. His words on blame, filed verbatim: *"We gotta stop using blame and medicine and start looking at the solution instead."* And his words on the games: *"Playing game has to end where the healing to begin."* (Transcription read as "blame *in* medicine" and "the playing games have to end where the healing begins" — his corrections open.)

Blame is one of the things in the between [H]: where medicine spends itself assigning fault — to patients, to doctors, to water, to places — it cannot spend itself fixing. The games — blame, lawsuits, politics — stop at the threshold; healing starts on the other side, and the line between them is the work [H]. This is why the two trees can get better *together*: the path opens when the blockers are named. The between is where the reading happens; it is also where the healing has to happen [H — his §168, read with his §178].

**Simply put.** It isn't only sickness standing between the doctor and the patient. Blame stands there too — pointing fingers at who caused what. His rule: put the finger down and pick up the fix. Nobody heals while everybody's blaming. The book's whole middle is about clearing that space so both trees can grow.

### The honest limits of the doctrine

Three honest lines hold the doctrine upright [H — his; D — the method]:

**First, the 100% is his.** Serology's honest limits — cross-reactivity, window periods, his own panel-rule partials — mean 100% detection isn't on offer. His "somehow" does honest work here: he claims the *connection* (read the between, know the ends), not the *mechanism*. The book holds the claim, not the 100% [H].

**Second, the panel rule governs the reading.** His standing rule, section 92, verbatim in spirit: *partial relationships must be looked upon if full ones cannot be seen.* Partials examined, never discarded, never upgraded. The between read honestly is a between read partially, and partials count [H].

**Third, the tested-showing thesis sits underneath.** Section 129, his words in substance: what shows most may just be the thing that was tested — the other items might not have been tested and looked for. Gaps are mainly incomplete testing that couldn't be done or couldn't be afforded — and good old medicine used to fill those gaps with the human intuition of the brain, which he is trying somehow to put on paper [H]. The doctrine is the prescription for that: test the between completely, and watch the ends resolve.

**Simply put.** Three guardrails on the doctrine itself: he doesn't claim the tests are perfect — he claims the *connection* and leaves the how open. Half-answers count as half-answers, never thrown away, never dressed up as whole ones. And remember that what you see most may just be what you looked for hardest. The old doctors knew this in their bones. He's writing down what they knew.

## Chapter 25 — The Trial

*Manuscript material for his clinical-research setting — a book's trial design, not a real trial, not medical advice, and no facilitation of human-subjects research beyond the manuscript design. The design below is the builder's formalization of his doctrine, labeled as such.*

Designed for his dream-job lab [H — his dictation, section 156, his words]: *"if I could have a dream job, I wish I could be the head hospitalist or the head hospital administrator at Mayo Clinic in Eau Claire, Wisconsin. And I know I could fix medicine for the world through that."* The upper-Midwest arm runs where the arboviruses live — La Crosse virus country, an hour from the virus's namesake town — and where his doctors are: he named Kelsey Meyer and Dr. Vandy Zandi, *"to spread the medicine and help me prove it in a clinical research setting"* [H — his words, his intention].

It is a *diagnostic-order* study, not a treatment trial: no drug tested, no therapy assigned — the same tests, run in his order, measuring what the order changes [D — the design].

**Simply put.** The book doesn't just preach the doctrine — it draws the test that would prove it. No new drugs, no experiments on people. Just: run the middle test first, keep everything else running too, and count what the middle test explains that nobody explained before. A reading-order test, in the hospital he hopes to run someday, in the valley where the virus named for a Wisconsin town lives.

### The rationale — the documented pieces

**The panels exist.** Mayo's ARBOP — Arbovirus Antibody Panel, IgG and IgM, Serum — covers California (La Crosse), St. Louis, Eastern equine, and Western equine encephalitis viruses by immunofluorescence assay, with individually orderable components and a CSF counterpart; Quest and other reference labs run equivalents [D]. The between is a blood draw. Douglas has lived this panel; his mother lived it [H — his account].

**The unknown-pneumonia burden is large and documented.** The failure to detect a pathogen in community-acquired pneumonia is the literature's most constant finding: ~30–60% of cases across studies (European Respiratory Society review) [D]; "as many as 50%" even when extensive testing has been performed (IDSA/ATS evidence review) [D]; 45.9% with no cause found in a systematic one-year hospitalized-CAP study using cultures, urinary antigens, viral PCR, and procalcitonin together [D]; 26.7–87.3% across European adult studies [D]. His "unknown pneumonia" is the literature's own category — CAP of unknown etiology. This is the benchmark the trial measures itself against.

**The brain is already in the lung's diseases.** His encephalitis-in-all-viruses study: 53 of 62 sickness-causing viruses with documented encephalitis (85.5%), 56 of 62 with any documented CNS involvement (90.3%); the arboviral families went 20-for-20 [D — his study's count]. Influenza → encephalitis, measles → SSPE, pneumococcus → meningitis, SARS-CoV-2 → encephalitis [D].

**The endemic mycoses ride the same showing.** 15–30% of CAP is Valley Fever in the endemic Southwest; 24% of it never tested for the fungus at all (Mayo Phoenix) [D]. Histoplasmosis wore TB's name for decades across the Ohio-Mississippi Valley [D]. The fungal column is still the emptiest column in every workup he has built — his 46 cells to fill [GAP — his].

**The nourishment leg.** His section 139 [H — his doctrine]: malnutrition → false-negative tests; nourishment restores; *feed the patient, retest*. The documented legs: albumin, prealbumin, BMI, and vitamin D are standard measured nutritional markers [D]; immune suppression — including the nutritional kind — degrades test sensitivity as a matter of immunology (the textbook case: malnourished patients go anergic, false-negative on TB skin tests and IGRAs) [D]. This leg decides the measurement-order question below.

**The vector's calendar.** Arboviral transmission in temperate regions is seasonal — mosquitoes active late spring through first frost, which is when La Crosse, West Nile, and the equine encephalitides move [D]. The trial runs one full vector season in its pilot phase for exactly this reason: the between is loudest when the vectors are working. Off-season CAP still enrolls (the doctrine doesn't hibernate), but the analysis stratifies by season — the manuscript wants to know whether the between sorts more in August than in January [H — his].

**The personal precedent.** He has lived the between-first reading himself: his September 2025 arbopanel (Calif [LaCrosse] IgG 1:20, the rest negative, Dr. Brown's "past exposure" reading with an ID referral placed) sits beside his October 2025 pneumococcal panel and his October 2025 sputum culture — three records, three providers, two lab systems, one sick human being [D — his records; H — his cross-reference]. His mother lived the panel before him — her positive panel inspired his own [H — his account]. The doctrine's testable form is, in one sense, the formalization of what his family already did: run the between, read what it says.

**Simply put.** Why run this test? Because the blood tests already exist at real labs. Because up to half of pneumonias never get a named cause — the literature's own number, not his. Because the lung bugs keep reaching the brain on the record. Because the Southwest's valley fungus shows up in a third of pneumonias and a quarter never even get tested for it. Because hungry bodies break tests, and mosquitoes keep a calendar. And because he already lived the between-first reading on his own blood, and his mother before him.

### The inverted workup, operationalized

**Population** [H — his design; the criteria [D]]: adults (≥18) presenting with community-acquired pneumonia by IDSA/ATS clinical criteria (new pulmonary infiltrate plus compatible clinical features) at hospitals in arbovirus-endemic regions — the Upper Midwest arm (La Crosse/Jamestown Canyon country) and the Southwest arm (Valley Fever country, where the fungal column of the workup runs beside the arboviral one). Exclusions (manuscript-level, his to refine): hospital-acquired pneumonia, known immunocompromise already on paper (the trial studies the *unlooked-for*, not the already-known), inability to consent.

**Step 0 — The nourishment baseline (first blood, first numbers).** At presentation, before the serology: albumin, prealbumin, BMI, and a documented nutritional screen [D]. This is his §139 operationalized — the patient's fed-state measured, not assumed. A malnourished patient tested first is a false-negative patient; the between-first workup fails on an unfed patient [H — his].

**Step 1 — The between, run first.** Full arboviral serology up front, composed per region. Upper Midwest: Mayo ARBOP or equivalent (California/La Crosse, St. Louis, Eastern and Western equine encephalitis viruses — IgG *and* IgM, serum, IFA), Jamestown Canyon virus serology (his arbopanel virus, the one he carries), West Nile virus IgG/IgM — with PRNT confirmation wherever California-serogroup cross-reactivity is suspected, because the documented 23% cross-reactivity means the screening panel proposes and PRNT disposes [D]. Southwest: the same arboviral core (the between is the between everywhere [H — his]), plus coccidioidal serology (immunodiffusion ID-TP/ID-CF and complement fixation titers) and histoplasma urine antigen where the overlap reaches [D]. IgM reads recent, IgG reads history — the panel's own vocabulary [D]. Partials (his panel rule, §92) are recorded as partials, examined, never discarded, never upgraded [H].

**Step 2 — The standard CAP workup, in parallel, not instead.** Blood cultures ×2, sputum culture, urinary antigens (pneumococcal, Legionella), respiratory viral PCR — the IDSA/ATS core [D]. The doctrine inverts the *order of reading*, not the standard of care: nothing standard is skipped. The standard workup is also the trial's measuring stick — "unknown etiology" is defined against it (no pathogen identified by Step 2's battery), so the between's contribution is measured, not assumed.

**Step 3 — The TB and fungal forks, triggered by the between.** Where the between reads positive or partial: TB workup (NAAT, culture, IGRA) and endemic-mycosis workup per region [D]. The between sorts; the forks confirm [H — his].

**Step 4 — Feed, then retest.** Where the nourishment baseline reads low: nutritional support per the treating team, then repeat the serology. *Feed the patient, retest* [H — his §139]. The retest is the doctrine's signature move — the showing changes when the patient is fed, and the trial measures the change.

**Simply put.** Five steps, in order: (0) check whether the patient is fed — first blood, first numbers; (1) run the brain-inflammation blood tests first — the middle read; (2) run the normal pneumonia workup anyway, at full speed — nothing skipped; (3) if the middle test points somewhere, chase it — TB tests, fungus tests; (4) if the patient was hungry, feed them and run the middle test again. The order of *reading* flips. The standard of care doesn't.

### His measurement-order question, answered three ways

His question: what gets measured first — the serology, the nourishment baseline, or something else? [H — his]

**The case for serology first.** The between is the doctrine's whole point — read the middle, know the ends. Serology first is the purest inversion: the blood's memory laid out before the cultures even grow [H].

**The case for the nourishment baseline first.** His own §139 decides it: malnutrition → false-negative tests. A serology drawn on a starved immune system is a serology that lies by omission — and the trial would then measure the *starvation*, not the between [H — his doctrine; D — the legs].

**The case for both in parallel.** One venipuncture, two tubes — the chemistry and the serology drawn together, the baseline read before the serology is interpreted. No delay, no lost time, the order preserved in the *reading* rather than the *drawing* [H — the builder's proposal, his to rule on].

**The deciding weight, stated plainly:** his §139. The fed-state has to be *known* before the first test is *believed*. Nourishment baseline first (or drawn with and read before); serology second; the standard workup in parallel throughout. The doctrine stays inverted — the between is still read before the ends — but it stands on fed ground. That is his own rule applied to his own trial [H].

The three orders, compared side by side:

| | Serology first | Nourishment first | Both in parallel |
|---|---|---|---|
| Speed to the between | Fastest — the doctrine's pure form [H] | Slower — baseline before blood [H] | Same draw, ordered reading [H] |
| Trust in the result | Lowest — unfed serology can lie by omission [D/H] | Highest — fed-state known first [H] | High — baseline read before serology interpreted [H] |
| Fidelity to the doctrine | Purest inversion [H] | Inversion with foundation [H] | Inversion in the reading [H] |

**The failure mode, narrated** [H — his]: serology-first on an unfed patient finds nothing — and the trial records a negative that belongs to the starvation, not the between. That is the exact error his §139 was written to prevent, and the exact error the literature's 30–60% unknown fraction may already contain: how many "unknown etiology" CAP cases were unknown because the patient was unfed when the tests were drawn? The trial cannot answer that retrospectively — but it refuses to repeat it prospectively. That refusal is the nourishment baseline.

**Simply put.** Which comes first — the middle blood test or the hunger check? His own rule settles it: a hungry body's tests can lie. So you check the hunger first (or draw both and read the hunger first), then you trust the middle test. Test the between on fed ground — or you'll measure the hunger and call it the answer.

### Endpoints — how "resolved" gets decided

**Primary** [H — his design; the benchmark [D]]: the proportion of "unknown etiology" CAP — no pathogen identified by the standard workup — given a name by the between-first workup. The literature's 30–60% unknown fraction is what the trial measures itself against.

**Secondary:** time from presentation to etiologic diagnosis; the fungal column (coccidioidal/histoplasma detection in the cohort); pneumococcal serotype distribution against arboviral serostatus (his §165 cross-referencing, run prospectively); the nourishment effect — serologic yield before vs. after nutritional support [H].

**What success looks like** [H — his bar]: the unknown fraction moves — a third or more of it resolved, the fungal column filling at the rates the untested 24% always implied.

**What failure looks like** [H — his]: the between adds nothing — the "resolved" column stays empty. That publishes too, with the same tier tags and the same prominence. A failed inversion is still a finding. His method demands the trial be allowed to lose.

**The adjudication** [H — his method: terms defined before use]: *Resolved* = a pathogen or etiology identified that the standard workup did not identify, attributed by a blinded panel of two physicians (one infectious-disease, one pulmonary — his §136 specialties). *Partially resolved* = the between proposes and the forks cannot dispose (a positive California-serogroup IgG with negative PRNT; a cocci CF titer below the diagnostic threshold) — recorded as partial per his panel rule, counted separately, never folded into "resolved." *Unresolved* = the between adds nothing — the trial's negatives, and they publish. The adjudicators read the between *first* and the standard workup *second* — the inversion applied to the analysis itself. Discordant adjudications go to a third reader; the disagreement rate is itself a reported result.

**Simply put.** The trial's one big question: of all the pneumonias nobody could name, how many does the middle test name? If a third or more get names, the doctrine earned its proof. If none do, that goes in the book too — just as loud. And "resolved" gets decided by two blinded doctors reading the middle test first, with half-answers counted as half-answers. No padding the score.

### Phases — the trial walks before it runs

**Phase 0 — The rehearsal (paper, no blood)** [H — the builder's extra, formalized as his §129 turned into a method]: 200 closed CAP charts from one endemic hospital, the between-first reading order applied to the tests actually ordered, the count of "unknowns" that already contained an unread between. If the charts say the between was there all along, Phase 1 is justified; if they don't, the doctrine takes its hit on paper, cheaply — his looking-bias protocol. Costs no blood; proves it on paper before proving it in blood. The rehearsal's count is his to run [GAP — his].

**Phase 1 — The pilot (one ward, one season).** Fifty consecutive consenting CAP admissions at the dream-job lab, one arbovirus season (late spring through first frost in the upper Midwest — the vector's calendar [D]). The inverted workup run exactly as specified; the adjudication panel reading blinded. Primary question: does the machinery work — can the between-first order run inside a real hospital's real workflow without slowing the standard of care? [GAP — operational details his to set]

**Phase 2 — The cohort (two regions, full seasons).** Upper Midwest + Southwest arms, powered on the 30–60% unknown fraction [D], the nourishment baseline and feed-retest loop running throughout [H]. The trial stops early for futility if the between adds nothing by the interim look — the negative publishes either way.

**Phase 3 — The manuscript (always).** Whatever the numbers say, they go into the book with their tier tags on — resolved, partial, unresolved, and the disagreement rate [H]. The trial's final product is not a journal paper first; it is a chapter of this book. The journal version follows if the numbers earn it. Either way, the doctrine will have been tested the way he tests everything: against the record, three ways, in public.

**Simply put.** Before touching a single live patient: read 200 old charts on paper and count how many mysteries already had the middle answer sitting unread in them. Then fifty patients, one mosquito season, one ward — does the machine run without slowing real care? Then the full two-region study. Then, whatever the numbers say, they go in the book with their labels on. Walk, then run, then tell the truth about both.

### Worked example: reading a between (his own panel, worked)

The manuscript works one between all the way through, so the trial's readers can see the reading done. His September 2025 arbopanel [D — his record]: **Calif (LaCrosse) Encep Ab, IgG: 1:20** (reference <1:10 — flagged abnormal), **IgM: <1:10.** EEE, SLE, WEE: IgG and IgM all <1:10.

**The standard reading** (Dr. Brown's note, her words): IgG positive reads as past exposure; IgM negative reads as no acute illness; "most consistent with a past exposure and/or illness"; ID referral placed [D — her note].

**The between-first reading** (his doctrine applied) [H]: the IgG is *memory* — the between saying "this ground has been crossed before." The IgM silence is *absence of the acute* — not absence of the between. The partial (one virus of four, one antibody class of two) is examined, never discarded, never upgraded. The between proposes La Crosse-country exposure; the forks it triggers are questions, not answers: what else has this ground seen — Jamestown Canyon? West Nile — which were not on this panel at all, a documented gap in the instrument itself [D].

**The nourishment read** [H — his §139]: his panel was drawn on whatever fed-state he was in that day — unmeasured, unrecorded. The trial's Step 0 exists because of exactly this silence: the between read without the baseline is a reading without its foundation.

*The example is not a diagnosis and not a claim about him — it is the method demonstrated on a document, the way his Shapes booklet works binomials. The trial teaches its readers to read the same way.*

**Simply put.** Read it like a story, not a verdict. The IgG says: this ground was crossed before. The silent IgM says: not crossed *right now*. One virus of four, one antibody of two — a half-answer, kept as a half-answer. And two whole viruses the panel never even asked about. Plus: nobody wrote down whether he was fed. That's the reading. That's what Step 0 fixes.

### Ethics and the charter

**Informed consent, first and always** [D — the standards; H — his charter]. His standing ethical inheritance is the Nuremberg Code — voluntary consent, the subject's right to withdraw, the experiment designed to yield fruitful results for the good of society [D]. Every participant consents in plain language; every participant can leave. The consent form states, in plain words: what the between-first order changes (nothing about your treatment — only the order the tests are read in), what extra blood is drawn (the serology tubes), and that leaving changes nothing about your care [H].

**The sickest are protected most** [D]. Enrollment never delays antibiotics, never delays oxygen, never delays anything the standard of care requires. The between-first order is a *reading* order — the standard workup runs at full speed regardless, and the trial measures the reading, not the patient.

**The doctor gets the guidance he asked for** [H — his §194]: *"the doctor is looking for some type of guidance in these situations once in a while."* The trial's deliverable is exactly that — a worked example of what the between-first order finds, written so a working doctor can use it. Not a mandate, not a guideline — guidance, the thing he watched doctors wish for.

**What the trial cannot claim** [D]. It cannot claim the between *causes* the pneumonia — serology reads exposure and memory, not causation. It cannot claim superiority over the standard workup without a randomized comparator. It cannot claim the fungal column without the fungal tests. Every claim the trial makes will carry its tier tag, the way his manuscript does.

**The negatives get published** [H — his looking-bias protocol]. A between-first workup that resolves *nothing* is still a result — the negative publishes, the doctrine takes the hit in public. The trial is not allowed to quietly bury a failed inversion. **No one is experimented on**: this is a diagnostic-order study on consenting adults receiving standard care throughout, and the manuscript's designs never leave the manuscript without his explicit word and an institution's review [H].

**The charter over the whole thing is his** [H — his words, section 121]: *"in the most responsible manner possible."* His four hats, as he names them: **patient, clinical researcher, aspiring medical technical publisher, and a friend of medicine.** The fixes aren't accusations — they're repairs, done responsibly. And his §143 research proposal stands beyond this design — microdose-medicine biome-shift studies in healthy people, *"then you can actually know what the hell you're doing with medicine"* [H — his words, his proposal, his to develop; filed, not facilitated].

**The lab doctor's chair is staffed** [H — his Fix List #1]: the trial's lab doctor owns the between — chooses the regional panel, orders the PRNT confirmations, reads the partials, runs the reflexes the ordering physician didn't know to ask for. The trial is, among other things, the working demonstration of his first fix — the chair filled, the work shown, the numbers kept.

**Simply put.** Nobody gets experimented on. Everybody signs on in plain language and can walk away. The sickest get their real treatment first, always — this only changes the *order the tests get read in*. If the middle test adds nothing, the book prints that failure in big letters. And the whole thing runs under his one-sentence charter: in the most responsible manner possible. That's not a slogan. It's the license the whole trial operates under.

## Chapter 26 — The First Five and the Fix List

*An informational model — manuscript reference, never medical advice. Testing decisions belong to the clinician.*

His commission, his words [H]: *"Show me a model of recommended testing that you would give for a person that was sick that didn't know what's wrong with them. Based upon the testing we have today. And start with the first five tests. That will be the cheapest to run that would tell us the most information."*

### The First Five — cheapest first, most information per dollar

The First Five are the sorting floor — commodity tests in every major reference lab's catalog [D]:

**1. CBC with differential — the blood's census** (~$25–50 self-pay) [D]: red cells, white cells with the differential, platelets — infection fighting (high whites), which arm is fighting (neutrophils leaning bacterial, lymphocytes leaning viral, eosinophils leaning parasitic/allergic), anemia, platelet trouble. His placement, section 146: the CBC is test #1 because it touches the reaction without detailing it [H].

**2. Comprehensive metabolic panel — the chemistry** (~$40–90) [D]: glucose, kidney, liver, electrolytes, calcium, protein — the organs' report card in one draw.

**3. CRP — the inflammation signal** (tens of dollars) [D]: C-reactive protein rises fast with inflammation and infection, falls fast when they ease — the "is something on fire, and is it going out" number.

**4. ESR — the slow inflammation signal** (tens of dollars) [D]: erythrocyte sedimentation rate, the older, slower marker — moves over days to weeks where CRP moves in hours. The pair separates the acute from the smoldering.

**5. Urinalysis — the kidney's report** (~$25–50) [D]: protein, blood, white cells, bacteria, glucose, ketones — kidney trouble, urinary infection, diabetic spillover, for the price of a cup.

**The five together: roughly $150–300 self-pay — two tubes and a cup** — and they answer the first questions of every sick-unknown: Is there infection? Which arm? Are the organs holding? Is there inflammation, acute or smoldering? Is the kidney in it? [D]

**Simply put.** Five cheap tests every lab on earth can run. Count the blood. Check the chemistry. Is something on fire (CRP)? Has it been smoldering a while (ESR)? What does the urine say? Two tubes of blood and a cup. About the price of a tank of gas. And those five answers tell the doctor which hallway to walk down next.

### After the five — the model, his forks

The five are the sorting floor; what they show picks the fork — and the forks are his pillars as tier two [H — his model]:

- **Infectious/inflammatory signals → the infectious fork:** blood cultures ×2, the arboviral panel (his between — encephalitis serology), the PN23M 23-serotype pneumococcal panel (his panel rule), the fungal workup, chest imaging where the lungs are in it [H].
- **The reflex principle — his Fix List #1** [H]: every abnormal result reflexes to its follow-up. The lab doctor's job: no wasted blood, no wasted testing.
- **The nourishment note — his §139** [H]: starved bodies break tests — feed the patient, retest. The five get re-run after nourishment, not just after treatment.
- **The panel rule — his §92** [H]: partials examined, never discarded, never upgraded. The five are the start of the record, not the verdict.

**Honest lines** [D]: prices move — the same CBC is priced from ~$3 to ~$40 across negotiated tiers, so the ranges above are walk-in self-pay, not quotes. This is the model's shape, not an order sheet. The clinician picks the tests.

**Simply put.** The five tests don't give the answer — they pick the next hallway. Infection signals? Walk the infectious hallway: cultures, the middle-test panel, the 23-serotype map, the fungus hunt, a chest picture. Weird result? It automatically triggers its follow-up — nobody's blood gets wasted. And remember the hunger rule: if the patient was starving when the tests ran, feed them and run the five again. Half-answers stay half-answers. The first five are the beginning of the record, not the end of it.

### The Fix List — his, numbered as dictated

The Fix List is his — his words, his items, numbered as dictated [H]. It opens with his charter, section 121, verbatim: *"in the most responsible manner possible."* The fixes aren't accusations — they're repairs, done responsibly, by a patient, a clinical researcher, an aspiring medical technical publisher, and a friend of medicine [H — his four hats, his words].

**Fix 1 — A lab doctor over every test** (his dictation, Oct 17, his words, verbatim) [H]: *"Laboratory medicine has to have a lab doctor dedicated to making certain every fucking test is activated properly and has a full reflexology done so there's no waste in blood or in testing you know to help the patient and save ultimately save Medicare and Medicaid money."*

His "reflexology" is read as **reflex testing** — the lab term: a result automatically triggering its follow-up test — his emphasis kept [H]. The fix: a dedicated lab doctor whose job is making certain every test is ordered properly and carries full reflex testing — the right test, activated correctly, with automatic follow-through where indicated. The purpose, twofold: **no waste** — no wasted blood drawn, no wasted testing run — **to help the patient**, and to **save Medicare and Medicaid money** ultimately [H].

**Simply put.** One doctor whose whole job is the tests. Not ordering more tests — owning the tests: the right test, run right, and every odd result automatically chasing its own follow-up. Nobody's blood drawn twice for what one draw could have answered. The patient helped. The public money saved. That's Fix 1.

**Fix 2 — his empty chair.** As dictated, the list holds one numbered fix and an open seat [H — his]: *"More fixes to be dictated. Each gets its own number."* The chair stays empty until he fills it. He said "remind me later" — so the book waits, and the emptiness is honest, not a placeholder pretending to be a fix. Nothing is drafted for him, nothing is guessed at. The seat is his, the number is reserved, and the book will re-prompt when he is ready [GAP — his].

**Simply put.** Fix 2 doesn't exist yet — and the book is proud of that. An empty chair with his name on it, waiting for his next dictation. The book doesn't guess what he'd say. It holds the seat open and reminds him later. Honest emptiness beats a filled-in fake.

That is the whole of Part 6: the between — the middle pillar read first, the trial that tests the reading, the five tests that start the workup, the lab doctor who owns it all — done in the most responsible manner possible. *With the hopes of saving the world in medicine together* [H — his closing line].
